# Amby sync: encrypted synchronization through your own server

Date: October 20, 2026.
Status as of 2026-11-05: **implemented: Э0–Э4б, appendix Е, and note properties per appendix З** (core, server, engine, app, conflicts v2, plans, password change; properties З1–З63 merged into `dev` on November 5, `cd01018`; the gates are passed, `GATES_PASSED = true`, the owner's server is running). **Not verified by hand:** property sync across two computers. **Not implemented:** Э2б (open registration, device name after sign-in; QR deferred until the mobile app), Э5 (versions and deleted files from the server in the app, space deletion from the app, streaming encryption of large files, `docs/sync-format.md`, security review). **Deferred by the owner on November 5:** monetization and everything tied to it (the paid "Amby Server", payment, sign-in with Google and Apple). What to do next and in what order: [road/target/sync.md](road/target/sync.md). The first step of the overall sync plan: "Personal" mode (whole files),
your own server in Docker, encryption on the device. A paid service, shared spaces with real-time
editing, phone and web are the following plans. Only what is needed so that nothing has to be redone later is included here.
Once code exists, the format and protocol rules will move into the contract `docs/sync-format.md`, and this file
will remain a plan. Decisions Р1–Р14 stand until they are explicitly changed. Promises О1–О8, decisions Р, and failures С1–С14 are requirements, and tests for each of them are written before the code (section 6).

## Public contract (November 13, 2026)

The implemented protocol and format v1 are described in [sync-format.md](sync-format.md); where the plan and the contract
disagree, the contract is right (it has been checked against the code). The discrepancies found while writing it are in
[road/target/sync-format-discrepancies.md](road/target/sync-format-discrepancies.md). The tables of error codes
(Б.0.1) and settings (Б.9) below were not extended with new rows: the full list is in the contract.

## Clarification of limits: file size (November 11, 2026, owner's decision)

The maximum source size of a single synced file is **30 MB (30,000,000 bytes)**.
Amby is a note-taking app; support for large files and lifting the size limit through streaming
encryption are not planned. This replaces the earlier goal of syncing files larger than the memory
limit in Э4/Э5 and the earlier target maximum of 512 MiB. The existing streaming open and the integrity
checks remain mandatory. The server limit for the encrypted body must account for the
overhead bytes on top of the permitted source size. Exceeding the limit does not delete the local
file: it is shown under "Не синхронизируется" (Not syncing) with a clear reason.

**Implementation in the current work:** the `EngineConfig.max_file_bytes` limit defaults to 30,000,000 bytes.
For the current format, the server body limit corresponding to a 30 MB source file is
30,416,160 bytes; the server cannot check the source length of an encrypted file. Historical mentions of large files below are to be read with this clarification.

## Open registration (November 11, 2026, owner's decision)

- **Рег1. Server mode.** `AMBY_SYNC_OPEN_REGISTRATION=1` enables account creation without an invitation;
  the default is `0`. `GET /v1/info` reports `registration: {open: boolean}`. An old server without this
  field is considered closed; the client does not guess the mode from the server address.
- **Рег2. Creation.** In the existing `POST /v1/auth/enroll`, a missing or empty `enroll_code`
  is allowed only in open mode and only for a new name under the rules of П11. The user, keys, and
  first device are created in one transaction. Concurrent requests for the same name create one account.
  Existing accounts and names reserved by an invitation are not replaced: `409 registration_unavailable`.
  A non-empty code is always checked under the previous invitation rules, even when registration is open.
- **Рег3. Attempt limiting.** The previous sign-in and invitation limits are kept. Open-registration
  attempts are additionally limited per IP regardless of the name: no more than `login_max_failures` per
  `login_window_secs` (by default 5 per 900 seconds), including successful registrations; after that, `429` with
  `Retry-After`. Spoofing `X-Forwarded-For` does not bypass the limit while `trust_proxy` is off.
- **Рег4. App.** An open server does not require an invitation code; for a reserved name, the explicit
  choice «У меня есть код приглашения» (I have an invitation code) remains. The recovery code is shown and confirmed
  before the registration is submitted. The password and the decrypted key are not sent to the server. An old client can still
  use invitations. QR and renaming of devices and the account are separate tasks.

- **Рег5. Quota of the current server** (owner's decision, November 11): new accounts get
  1 GB (1,000,000,000 bytes) in total of encrypted current files and unfinished uploads.
  The owner's previous account keeps its previous overall quota through a pinned plan. History is counted
  separately under the server's current rules.

## Connection screen (November 11, 2026, owner's decision)

One form: at the top the "Sign in / Register" switch and the account fields, at the bottom the server choice.
The separate steps for choosing the action and checking the server are removed. Availability is checked automatically
without sending the password or registering; the indicator sits next to the server choice. For a custom address, the check
is deferred until a pause in typing; a response from the previous address does not change the state of the new one. Until the current
server has been checked successfully, the form cannot be submitted. The form has one primary button, «Войти» (Sign in) or
«Зарегистрироваться» (Register). Password recovery remains a link; the recovery-code confirmation
during registration and the previous invitation rules are kept.

## Case-insensitive login (November 12, 2026, owner's decision)

ASCII case is ignored: sign-in, registration, recovery,
renaming, the availability check, and administrator commands fold `A–Z` to `a–z`.
The server stores and returns the canonical lowercase name; case variants do not create
separate accounts and do not bypass the sign-in attempt limit. Unicode and the previous rules for
permitted characters are not extended. Existing lowercase names and keys are not changed.

## Account and device names (November 11, 2026, owner's decision)

The account name is the login. Changing it requires the current password and does not change the AK, the recovery
pair, spaces, quotas, or existing connections. The new name follows the
previous login rules; a taken name is rejected. GET `/v1/account/username` returns the
current login by token; PUT accepts `username`, `base_username`, `auth_key`.
A wrong password proof is `403 invalid_proof` with the previous attempt limit,
a taken name is `409 username_taken`, a stale base is `409 username_conflict`.
Repeating an already completed rename to the current name succeeds. After the change, sign-in and
recovery work with the new login, and other devices update the label when they
read the device list; an old server without this API keeps working.

PUT `/v1/devices/{device_id}` accepts only the encrypted `name_enc` of the previous
fixed format. Devices of one's own account can be changed; a foreign or missing
identifier gives 404. Tokens, identifiers, creation dates, and keys do not change.
A device name is non-empty, contains no control characters, and takes at most 64 UTF-8 bytes.
The password is not stored in the connection record and does not go into the log; the local login is updated
only after the server confirms. A retry lets the local write finish after a failure.

## 1. Goal and boundaries

The goal of the first version: two computers with Amby synchronize one vault through a server that the
user runs themselves in Docker. The server cannot read the content, the file names, or the
folder structure.

Included:

- a Rust encryption and sync core with no dependency on Tauri (the phone, the service, and the web will take it later);
- the server: one executable, a Docker image, and a sample `docker-compose` with HTTPS;
- the desktop app: connection, status, conflict list, recovery code.

| Not included                     | What instead                                                              |
| -------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| payment, accounts on our service | stage 3 of the overall plan; the server already has places for them (Р11) |
| shared spaces, real-time editing | a separate Yjs prototype; here only a separate key per space (Р14)        |
| automatic text merge             | on conflict both versions are kept (Р7); merge comes in Э4б (К1)          |
| phone, web                       | the core works with files, network, and time through interfaces (Р12)     |
| selective folder sync            | later; for now the whole vault is synced except service files (Р4)        |
| `.obsidian/`, `.git/`            | we do not touch other tools' settings and repositories                    |

## 2. What we promise the user

These promises will go into the interface and the documentation in plain words. The code must fulfil them.

- **О1. The server cannot read your data.** Neither ours nor your own. Notes, attachments, and file and folder
  names are encrypted on the device before sending and are decrypted only on your devices.
- **О2. What the server does see:** the user name, how many files you have, their approximate size, when they
  changed, and from which devices and IP addresses the changes come. This cannot be hidden, and we say so plainly.
- **О3. Password and recovery code.** The password is needed to connect a new device. The recovery code is for
  when the password is forgotten. **If both the password and the code are lost, nobody can recover the data on the server: neither we
  nor the server owner.** The files on your devices remain.
- **О4. Sync deletes nothing irrecoverably.** Before a file is overwritten, a snapshot is saved in
  the history. A file deleted on another device goes to the vault trash. The server keeps
  previous versions for 30 days.
- **О5. When two devices edit at the same time, both versions are kept.**
- **О6. If sync is about to delete or overwrite many files at once, it stops and
  asks.**
- **О7. File content does not change.** What is written on one device arrives on another byte for byte.
  The vault remains an ordinary folder, compatible with Obsidian.
- **О8. An honest caveat:** if the password is weak, whoever has a copy of the server can brute-force it on their own
  computer. That is why the password must be long and the check is deliberately slow (Argon2id).

## 3. Threat model

We defend against:

- reading of data by the server owner or host, and leaks of its database and backups;
- traffic interception (HTTPS plus encryption before sending);
- tampering with data by the server: forging content, swapping files with each other, forging deletion or
  renaming, and slipping an old version of a file to a device that has already seen a newer one.

We do not defend against the following, and we say so openly:

- an infected or unlocked device: the keys are on it;
- brute-forcing a weak password from a copy of the server (О8);
- the metadata from О2;
- the server can withhold new changes or show a new device an entirely old state.
  Devices that are already connected will notice a rollback of an individual file (Р6), but not a "freeze" of the whole space;
- a disconnected device keeps everything it managed to receive;
- the server can lose data. That is why a full copy always sits on every device.

## 4. Decisions

### Р1. The server stores blindly, all logic is on the device

The server accepts and returns encrypted records, keeps version numbers, rejects stale changes, and
reports what has changed. It does not need to understand the content, and it cannot. Change detection, comparison,
conflicts, encryption, and safe writing to disk are done on the device. Hence the order of work:
the complex and risky part is the core on the client, and the server is small.

### Р2. The unit of sync is a file, the path is hidden in the encrypted header

Every file and every folder is a record (item) with a random 128-bit ID. A record consists of an
encrypted **header** (path, type, size, content hash, reference to the body) and an encrypted
**body** (the file content). Folders have no body; folder records exist so that empty folders arrive too.

The path is stored as a string, not as a tree of parents. This is simpler and more reliable for conflicts. Renaming a
folder is a batch of path changes for all its files in one atomic commit (Р3): other devices never
see a half-renamed folder. A thousand short headers in one request is an acceptable price. Up to 10,000 files, a folder rename is atomic (one commit); if there are more files, it goes in several commits, and other devices may briefly see an intermediate state: the Э3 engine survives this. (Clarified by Т4, November 13: the client sends commits of no more than 1,000 records and 1 MiB, so it is atomic up to 1,000 files.)

Records do not change file content (О7): the body is the exact bytes of the file, including the BOM and line endings.

### Р3. Versions and write "only if nobody got there first"

- Every record has a version number. The new version is always "base + 1".
- A commit carries a base version for each record. The server accepts the batch only if **all** base
  versions match the current ones; otherwise it rejects it as a whole and names the conflicting records. The batch is
  applied in a single SQLite transaction.
- Every accepted commit gets the next number in the space's change feed (`seq`). A device
  asks "what changed after `seq` N".
- A commit has a random `commit_id`. A retry after a dropped connection returns the previous result and is not
  applied twice.
- Time on the devices and on the server **never** decides which version takes precedence. Only version numbers do.

### Р4. What is synced

The whole vault is synced, except:

- `.git/`, `.obsidian/`, `.trash/` (the Obsidian trash);
- Amby temporary files (`.<name>.amby-tmp-*`) and system junk (`.DS_Store`, `._*`, `Thumbs.db`,
  `desktop.ini`);
- symbolic links, names that are not UTF-8, and files larger than the server limit: they are not synced and
  are shown in the "not syncing" list;
- everything in `.amby/` except an explicit list.

Of `.amby/`, only the documented important files are synced: `properties.json`, `blocks/`,
`graph-layout.json`, `property-backups/`, and (Э4б, Г24) `conflicts/`: the second versions of conflicts, so that a conflict is visible and can be resolved from any device. Not synced are `notes.db*` (the index is rebuilt from the
files), `history/`, `recovery/`, and `trash/` (each device has its own), `sync/` (the sync state of
this device), and anything not named. **A rule for the future:** every new file in `.amby/` states in
[vault-format.md](vault-format.md) whether it is synced; by default it is not. This way an unknown
file does not travel to other devices, but is not lost either.

### Р5. Keys

```text
password ─Argon2id(salt, 64 MiB, t=3, p=4)─▶ master key ─HKDF─┬─▶ auth_key  (goes to the server for sign-in)
                                                              └─▶ KEK       (never leaves the device)
recovery code (256 bits) ─HKDF─┬─▶ recovery_auth_key
                               └─▶ recovery_KEK
KEK          ─encrypts─▶ account key AK (random, 256 bits)
recovery_KEK ─encrypts─▶ the same AK
AK           ─encrypts─▶ space key VK (random, each vault has its own, with an epoch number)
VK ─HKDF─▶ header key;  VK ─HKDF(body_id)─▶ body key
```

- **Why the intermediate AK.** A password change re-encrypts one key, not the whole vault. Several
  vaults for one user and future access for other participants (Р14) also go through AK and VK.
- **The server stores:** the salt and the Argon2id parameters, the hashes of `auth_key` and `recovery_auth_key` (SHA-256: the keys are
  random anyway), the AK encrypted twice, and each space's VK encrypted by AK. It cannot read any
  key.
- **The device stores** AK and the device token in the system password store (`keyring`, service
  `com.ambynotes.sync`, as is already done for AI keys in `src-tauri/src/credentials.rs`). Not in the settings and
  not in the vault. This is **one record** `credentials` with a small JSON `{"v":1,"account_key":…,"device_token":…}`
  (`BundledSecrets` in `sync/secrets.rs`): one record means one macOS password prompt, not two. The record is read
  once per launch. The previous two records (`account-key`, `device-token`) are migrated like this: no new record →
  read both → write the new one → read it back and compare → only then delete the old ones; any error
  leaves the old ones in place and sign-in works in this launch. A new record takes precedence over leftovers of the old ones (they are not read);
  an unintelligible new one (not JSON, a different version) is not overwritten by reading: the old ones are used if they exist,
  otherwise an error, and a fresh sign-in replaces it. Sign-out deletes everything, the old names before the new record, so that
  a leftover does not come back to life. On password prompts in development and in release: `docs/dev-signing.md`. If there is no system store (some Linux systems), the password is asked on every launch.
- **Password:** at least 12 characters, with a lowercase and an uppercase letter (Unicode), a digit and a special character, and with a sufficient strength estimate (zxcvbn). The rule applies only to new passwords (creation, recovery, change); signing in with an existing password does not check it. The Argon2id parameters
  are recorded next to the salt and can be raised later without losing data.
- **Recovery code:** 256 random bits as groups of 4 characters (Crockford base32 with a
  checksum). Sync is not enabled until the user has saved the code (file or printout) and
  confirmed it by entering part of the code.
- **First device:** the server administrator creates a user with a command and gets a one-time code
  valid for 24 hours. The device enters the server address, the name, and the code, and sets a password. It creates the salt, AK, VK, and
  recovery code itself and sends the server only encrypted data and hashes.
- **New device:** sign in with the password (`auth_key`) → device token → decrypt AK.
- **Forgotten password:** sign in with `recovery_auth_key` → decrypt AK with the code → new password: new salt, AK
  re-encrypted, new `auth_key` hash.
- **Password change** on a connected device requires the old password. The other devices keep
  working; optionally they can be disconnected.
- **Disconnecting a device** revokes its token on the server. What it received before stays on it (section 3).
  Changing VK with a new epoch is groundwork; in the first version the epoch is always 1.

### Р6. Cryptography

Only ready-made, proven RustCrypto libraries: `chacha20poly1305` (XChaCha20-Poly1305 and the STREAM
streaming mode, audited by NCC Group), `argon2`, `hkdf` + `sha2`, `blake3` for content hashes,
`zeroize` for wiping keys in memory, and randomness from the OS (`getrandom`). There are no custom schemes, only the
combination described here. Test vectors come from the standards and from an independent Python reference (section 6). An independent audit is needed before the paid service;
for one's own server it is desirable but does not block.

The **header** is JSON with the fields `format: 1`, `version`, `kind` (`file` | `folder`), `path` (NFC, separator
`/`, relative to the vault root), `size`, `mtime_ms` (for reference only), `content_hash` (BLAKE3),
`body_id`, `deleted`. Unknown fields from newer Amby versions are preserved on rewrite. Before
encryption the JSON is padded to a multiple of 256 bytes so as not to give away the path length. Cipher:
XChaCha20-Poly1305 with a random 24-byte nonce. Associated data (AAD): `amby-sync/header/1`, the ID of the
space, the record ID, the key epoch, and `version`. So the server cannot swap headers between
records or pass off an old header as a new version.

The **body** is `AMBYBODY`, the format version, the key epoch, a 19-byte random nonce prefix, then chunks of
64 KiB in STREAM mode (each has an authentication tag, the last one is marked). The body key is
`HKDF(VK, "amby-sync/body/1" ‖ body_id)`, with a new `body_id` on every upload. Before encryption the
content is padded with zeros to the Padmé size: this hides the exact size, and the extra bytes are no more than 12%.
After decryption it is checked that BLAKE3 matches the `content_hash` from the header and that the padding is zeros.
On any error **nothing is written to disk.**

**Deletion** is also an encrypted header with `deleted: true`. The server cannot forge either deletion or
renaming. The server's open "deleted" mark is needed only for cleaning up old data; the device
trusts only the header.

**Rollback protection:** the device remembers the highest version of each record. If the server returns a lower one or the
version in the header does not match the claimed one, the record is not applied, sync stops, and
shows a warning.

### Р7. Conflicts

| What happened on two devices      | Result                                                                                                            |
| --------------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| both changed the content          | the path stays with whoever reached the server first; the second one's version sits next to it as a conflict copy |
| one renamed, the other changed    | merges by itself: the new name and the new content                                                                |
| both renamed differently          | the name from the server stays; the content is the same, nothing is lost                                          |
| one deleted, the other changed    | the edit wins, the file is restored                                                                               |
| both created a file with one path | the second becomes a conflict copy                                                                                |

- The conflict copy is named by the rule already adopted in Amby: `Name.<random-id>-conflict.md`. It is
  uploaded as a new file, so both versions are on all devices. The overwritten version also
  remains as a snapshot in the history.
- If a note is open and unsaved, the sync write is, for the editor, an ordinary external edit.
  The existing version comparison dialog fires: autosave is paused, and the choice is the user's.
- The "rename + edit" merge is done field by field relative to the last common version (Р10).
  Text inside a file was not merged in the first version; from stage Э4б (К1) `.md` notes are merged by lines and
  blocks relative to the base, and the second version in a real conflict lies in `.amby/conflicts/`, not next
  to the file (appendix Г replaces those points of this section that speak of a copy next to the file).
- `properties.json` and the database manifests obey the same rule. Amby keeps reading the main
  file, and the **conflict list** in the interface offers "keep mine" or "keep the other". Merging
  `properties.json` by note keys is appendix Ж, which is replaced by appendix З: values in the frontmatter, presentation by groups with merging by notes (database manifests remain a set-level conflict).
- Every conflict stays in the list until it is resolved.
- **To check at Э4:** the conflict copy has the same `amby-id` as the original, and the protection against duplicate IDs
  forbids some saves ([vault-format.md](vault-format.md#frontmatter-amby-id)). If this makes
  the original stop being editable, the losing version is kept in `.amby/sync/conflicts/` and is visible only
  in the conflict list. The decision is made in code, before the interface.

### Р8. Deletion and protection against mass loss

- A deletion that arrives from another device always moves the file to the vault trash `.amby/trash/`,
  whatever deletion mode is selected (`files.deleteMode`). This way it can be restored inside Amby.
- A deletion is applied only if the local file has not changed since the last sync. Otherwise the
  edit wins (Р7).
- Before a file is overwritten with the version from the server, a snapshot is always taken in the history with the reason `sync-overwrite`.
  It is not merged with autosave snapshots and is taken even if history is turned off: like
  `note-rewrite`.
- The server keeps previous versions and deleted records for 30 days (configurable). Restoring goes through the
  client: the server itself cannot decrypt them.
- **Stop on mass changes.** If one sync pass would delete or overwrite at least
  20 files and more than 10% of the synced ones, it stops and shows a list. The same for outgoing
  deletions: an accidentally deleted folder does not travel to all devices without a question.
- **The vault folder is missing or empty** (for example, an external drive is disconnected) while the state has files:
  sync stops. Disappearance is not deletion.
- **The server was reset** (its `instance_id` changed, or the space's `seq` is lower than the device knows):
  sync stops. An empty server is **never** treated as a command to delete local files.
  The user is offered to upload the vault again from this device.
- **Multi-file operations.** While the device has an unfinished log (`.amby/recovery/` for database links,
  the creative workshop, and so on), outgoing changes are not sent. A half-completed
  operation must not travel to other devices.
- A batch arriving from the server is applied with merged file-watcher events and a single
  index update. Applying can be repeated: after a failure it continues from the same place.

### Р9. File names on different systems

- Paths in headers are stored in NFC: macOS returns names in NFD, and without normalization one file
  would turn into two.
- Before writing to disk, it is checked that the name is valid on this system: characters forbidden on Windows
  `<>:"\|?*`, trailing dots and spaces, `CON`, `PRN`, `AUX`, `NUL`, `COM1`–`COM9`, `LPT1`–`LPT9`, and path length.
  A case-insensitive match on macOS and Windows is also checked (`Note.md` and `note.md`).
- If the name cannot be created, the file does not appear here. It goes to the "not syncing" list with an
  explanation and stays on the server and other devices. The user renames it where it is
  visible. There are no silent renames.

### Р10. Sync state on the device

- `.amby/sync/state.db` (SQLite) stores: the device and space IDs, the server address, the server's `instance_id`,
  the last `seq`, and for each item the path, type, hash, size and `mtime_ns` of the last shared version,
  the version on the server, `body_id`, unfinished uploads, the list of conflicts and the "not synced" list.
- This is not a cache, but it is not the only copy of the data either. If the file is lost, the device re-reconciles everything by
  hashes: matching files simply get linked, differing ones become conflict copies, nothing is deleted. It will
  be noisy, but safe.
- The device ID is also stored in the app data. If they do not match, the vault was copied from another
  computer: the device connects as a new one and does a full reconciliation instead of impersonating the other one. The switch is also reset in this case: a copied vault is turned on again explicitly (Opus decision, 23.10; the obsolete confirmation was removed 27.10).
- The wrapped space key (VK under AK, as on the server) is stored in `state.db` so that the vault starts without a network (Opus decision, 23.10).
- Changes on disk are found the same way as in the index: a quick check by size and `mtime_ns`, and on a
  mismatch a new BLAKE3 hash. Plus file watcher events and a full check at startup.
- A lock file prevents two processes from syncing the same vault at the same time.

### Р11. Server

- Rust, `axum`, SQLite (`rusqlite`, as in the app). Bodies are stored as files in `/data/blobs/`, split across
  directories. The body storage and user accounting are hidden behind interfaces so that a paid service can plug in
  S3 and billing without a rewrite.
- **What the server knows in the clear:** user names, devices (their names are encrypted with AK), spaces,
  item IDs and versions, header and body sizes, change times, which device made a commit.
- **Sign-in:** a one-time code from the administrator (`amby-sync-server user add <name>`), then a password. The device
  token is 256 random bits, and the server stores only its SHA-256. Sign-in attempts are rate-limited.
  Tokens, keys and `auth_key` never end up in logs.
- **Notifications:** Server-Sent Events at `/events`; if the connection does not hold, polling once every 60 seconds.
- **Limits:** a file up to 512 MiB by default (configurable). Upload in 8 MiB parts with resumption. Bodies without a
  commit are deleted after 24 hours. Quotas at the administrator's discretion, off by default.
- **HTTPS is required.** Plain HTTP is allowed only for `localhost` or via an explicit "on the
  local network" switch with a warning.
- **Docker:** multi-stage build, non-root run, a `/data` volume, a health check, settings via
  environment variables. An example `docker-compose` with Caddy, which obtains the certificate itself.
- **Server backup:** `amby-sync-server backup` (an online copy of SQLite and the bodies) or a copy of `/data` with the server
  stopped. The contents of the backup are encrypted, only the metadata is open.

First-version API (JSON over HTTPS):

| Request                                                            | Purpose                                                                             |
| ------------------------------------------------------------------ | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `GET /v1/info`                                                     | server version, protocol and format versions, limits, `instance_id`                 |
| `POST /v1/auth/prelogin`                                           | salt and Argon2id parameters for a name (plausible fake ones for nonexistent names) |
| `POST /v1/auth/enroll`                                             | the first device by a one-time code                                                 |
| `POST /v1/auth/login`, `POST /v1/auth/recover`                     | sign-in by password or by recovery code → device token                              |
| `GET` / `PUT /v1/keys`                                             | the encrypted AK (password change, recovery), with a version check                  |
| `GET /v1/devices`, `DELETE /v1/devices/{id}`                       | list of devices, disconnecting                                                      |
| `GET` / `POST /v1/spaces`                                          | spaces with encrypted names and VK                                                  |
| `GET /v1/spaces/{id}/changes?since=…`                              | change feed: headers and versions                                                   |
| `GET /v1/spaces/{id}/events`                                       | notifications about new commits                                                     |
| `POST /v1/spaces/{id}/blobs`, `PUT …/parts/{n}`, `POST …/complete` | uploading a body in parts                                                           |
| `GET /v1/spaces/{id}/blobs/{blob_id}`                              | downloading a body, with Range support                                              |
| `POST /v1/spaces/{id}/commit`                                      | an atomic batch of item changes (Р3)                                                |
| `GET /v1/spaces/{id}/items/{item_id}/versions`                     | earlier versions of an item (for restoring)                                         |

### Р12. Where the code lives

- A separate Cargo workspace `sync/`: `sync/proto` (types and formats), `sync/core` (cryptography and the sync
  engine), `sync/server` (the server and `Dockerfile`). Separate, so as not to touch the desktop workspace’s `target/`,
  the app's scripts and CI and so that the server builds in Docker without Tauri dependencies.
- `sync/core` works with files, network, time and randomness only through interfaces. That way it can be
  run in the simulation (section 7) and later hooked up to the phone and the web.
- `src-tauri` pulls in `sync/proto` and `sync/core` by path. A new module `src-tauri/src/sync/` ties the
  core to the existing vault services: atomic write, history snapshots, trash, the file
  watcher, the system key store. IPC commands with Specta annotations, the bindings are regenerated by
  `npm run rust:test`.
- **Sync writes and the watcher.** A sync write must not come back to the server as a new
  local edit. But the editors must learn about it, as about an external edit. Suppressing its own
  events for sync differs from the usual one: check this separately.
- The interface is an optional module `amby.sync`, off by default (as intended by the modular
  direction): a settings section, a status icon, a conflict list, a "not synced" list, a
  connection wizard with a recovery code. All strings are in `src/locales/resources.ts`.

### Р13. Version compatibility

- `/v1/info` reports the protocol and format versions. If a header or body version is newer than the
  client understands, it switches to "read-only" mode for those items and asks to update Amby. It does not
  rewrite items it does not understand.
- Unknown header fields are preserved on rename and other changes.
- The server does not need to understand headers, so new fields do not require updating it.

### Р14. Groundwork for the next stages

- **Shared spaces:** each space already has its own VK with an epoch. Later the account will get a key
  pair X25519, and the VK can be passed to a member. When a member is removed, the space gets a new
  epoch.
- **Paid service:** the same server plus body storage in S3, accounts and billing behind the same interfaces.
  Registration by email and password; sign-in through Google or Apple is possible (the owner's decision, 20 October
  2026), but only for sign-in and payment: it **never replaces** the encryption secret. The key is still
  unlocked only by the password, a connection from one's own device via QR (Э2б) or the recovery code, otherwise the
  service could read the data (О1).
- **Phone and web:** a core with no platform dependencies (Р12).

## 5. What happens on failures

| №   | Situation                                                             | What happens                                                                                                               |
| --- | --------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| С1  | the connection dropped during a body upload                           | resumption from the last part; the server deletes a body without a commit after 24 hours                                   |
| С2  | the connection dropped after the commit, before the state was written | a retry with the same `commit_id` returns the earlier result; in the feed the device recognizes its own commit by its hash |
| С3  | a failure while writing a downloaded file                             | the write is atomic: on disk there is either the old or the new file in full; after a restart it continues                 |
| С4  | the disk is out of space                                              | the atomic write fails, the file is untouched, the error appears in the status                                             |
| С5  | the downloaded data is corrupted or forged                            | authentication fails, nothing is written to disk, a retry, then an error                                                   |
| С6  | the server returned an old version of a file                          | stop and a warning (Р6)                                                                                                    |
| С7  | the server lost data or was reset                                     | stop; local files are not deleted; an offer to upload everything again (Р8)                                                |
| С8  | the disk with the vault is disconnected                               | stop, the disappearance is not treated as a deletion (Р8)                                                                  |
| С9  | a file is edited while it is being read for sending                   | the next check will notice the new change and send it                                                                      |
| С10 | the clock on the device drifted                                       | affects nothing: only versions decide (Р3)                                                                                 |
| С11 | the vault was copied to another computer together with `.amby/`       | connects as a new device, full reconciliation (Р10)                                                                        |
| С12 | wrong password on a new device                                        | the server does not issue a token; nothing is downloaded                                                                   |
| С13 | an old Amby met an item in a new format                               | read-only for it and a request to update (Р13)                                                                             |
| С14 | two windows or processes on one vault                                 | only one syncs (lock file)                                                                                                 |

## 6. Working order: tests first

The owner's decision (20 October 2026): all tests are written **before** the code. They check this plan, not an already
working system, so they cannot quietly adapt to implementation errors.

### Steps of each stage

1. **Interface.** Public types, traits and function signatures with `todo!()` bodies, no logic. In the
   doc comment of each function it is stated which requirements it fulfills (`Р8`, `С5`).
2. **Tests.** Written by a separate Sonnet session that has only this plan and the interface. Each test
   refers to a requirement in a comment (`// О4, Р8: a deletion from another device goes to the trash`).
   The tests check what is visible from outside (files on disk, server responses, bytes on the server), not
   internal structures.
3. **Red check.** All tests build and **fail** on the stubs, and for the right reason:
   a `todo!()` panic or a failed assertion, not a build error. A test that passes on a stub
   checks nothing, and it is rewritten. The result of the run is recorded in the stage report.
4. **Test review.** Opus checks the set against the plan: every requirement of the stage has a test, the tests are not
   tied to the code's structure, there are no tests that would pass on any implementation. The script
   `npm run sync:req-check` checks that every requirement О, Р and С from this plan is mentioned in at least
   one test (except Р12 and Р14: they are about the code's structure and the future).
5. **A separate commit with the tests.** The history shows that the tests appeared before the code.
6. **Code.** Another Sonnet session writes the implementation until the tests pass. **The tests must not be changed.** If a
   test seems wrong, work stops. Fixing a test is a separate commit with an explanation,
   after Opus review, and if it changes a promise from section 2, after the owner's agreement.
7. **Test strength check.** `cargo-mutants` introduces small breakages into the finished code (changes a condition,
   removes a check) and sees whether the tests fail. Every surviving breakage is examined: either a new test
   (first red on that breakage, in a separate commit), or a note on why it is harmless. For
   cryptography and the engine the goal is no unexplained surviving breakages.

### Rigor by risk

The owner's decision (20 October 2026). The full order of steps 1–7 (tests written by a separate session,
Opus review before the code, mutation testing, an independent reference where bytes are needed) is for what
loses or exposes data when it goes wrong: cryptography (Э1), the server (Э2), QR connection (Э2б) and the engine
(Э3). For the interface and the glue with the app (Э4) the order is lighter: tests are still written before
the code and go in the first commit, but one session may write both them and the code, Opus reviews once,
mutations and references are not required. The connection screen (wizard, password, recovery code, sign-in)
depends only on Э1 and Э2, so it is built in parallel with Э3.

### Branches and merging into `dev`

Decision (Opus, 20 October 2026). Sync work lives in `sync/*` branches and is merged into
`dev` only when the stage's tests are green. Merge with a regular merge, without squash: the history must keep the
commit with the tests before the code. Nothing red goes into `dev`, `beta` or `main`. `sync/` is not added to
`scripts/dev-only-paths.txt`: this is product code. Before merging Э1, remove `continue-on-error` from
`cargo test` in `.github/workflows/sync.yml`; `req-check` keeps it until the end of all stages.

### Where a test cannot be written simply from the plan, and what to do instead

- **Test vectors.** The expected bytes cannot be taken from code that is not written yet, and if they are taken from
  written code, the code will be checking itself. So the vectors for primitives come from standards: RFC 9106
  for Argon2id, RFC 5869 for HKDF, the IRTF draft for XChaCha20-Poly1305, the official BLAKE3 vectors,
  the Padmé values from the authors' paper. Vectors for our own format (key wrappings, header, body, recovery
  code) come from a small independent reference implementation in Python (`sync/reference/`,
  libsodium via PyNaCl), written from this plan before the Rust code. Rust must match it byte for byte.
- **Engine simulation (Э3).** The simulation itself is test code, and it too must be checked before the engine. For this
  several deliberately broken mini-engines are written: "the last one overwrites without a copy", "deletion beats an edit",
  "an empty server means delete everything", "does not notice a version rollback". The simulation must
  catch each one. Only then can it be trusted to check the real engine.
- **How the interface looks.** Behavior (a conflict appeared in the list, "keep mine" resolved it)
  is checked by tests before the code, by element identifiers agreed at the interface step. How it looks is
  checked by screenshots afterwards: it cannot be described by a test in advance.
- **Manual testing on two computers** is written as a scenario with the expected result before the code, following the
  model of [native-ui-smoke.md](native-ui-smoke.md).

### Stages

**Э0. Skeleton and test tooling.** The `sync/` workspace (`proto`, `core`, `server`, `conformance`),
the AGPL-3.0-only license, `cargo-deny` (licenses and vulnerabilities), a CI job, the commands `npm run sync:test`,
`sync:lint`, `sync:req-check` and `sync:mutants`, `cargo-fuzz`, an environment for the Python reference. Done
when the empty crates build in CI and `sync:req-check` honestly reports that no requirement is yet
covered.

**Э1. Cryptography** (`sync/core`).

- Tests before code: vectors from the standards; the Python reference and the vectors of our own format from it;
  property tests (encrypt and decrypt — get the same thing back; tampering with any byte, swapping
  headers between items, a foreign `body_id`, an old version, a wrong password or code are rejected;
  size is hidden within Padmé limits); fuzzing targets for header and body parsing.
- Code: the key hierarchy, AK and VK wrappings, client-side sign-in and recovery, headers, bodies in
  STREAM mode, Padmé, the recovery code.
- Done when the tests are green, the match with the reference is byte for byte, an hour of fuzzing without crashes, and there are no
  unexplained surviving breakages.

**Э2. Server and Docker.**

- Tests before code: the server conformance suite `sync/conformance` for the API from Р11. It works against any
  address, so it can later check both a self-hosted server and the future paid one. It includes commit races,
  rejection of a whole batch, a retry with the same `commit_id`, the change feed, resuming parts, cleanup, token
  revocation, sign-in attempt limiting, `instance_id`. The "server is blind" check: after the run, neither the
  server's database nor its bodies contain a single string from the test data. A script: build the image, start it,
  run the suite against the container.
- Code: the server, SQLite, body storage, administrator commands, `Dockerfile`, `docker-compose` with Caddy,
  instructions for a self-hosted server.
- Done when the suite passes both against the in-process server and against the container.

**Э2б. Open registration and QR connection** (the owner's decision, 20 October 2026; strict).

- Open registration on one's own server: a setting, off by default; when on, it lets a person
  sign up in Amby with just fields (name and password), without an invitation code. Protection against guessing is the same as
  for sign-in (П16).
- Connecting a new device by QR from an old one: the old one shows a code, the new one scans it and receives
  AK directly, without a password; the server only relays encrypted data and can read nothing (О1).
  Intermediary requests on the server (П43…) and an encryption function in the core (Ф35…) are needed; the key agreement method
  is chosen at step 0 and reviewed by Opus.
- Protocol change (Opus decision, 21.10, from the Э4 connection screen): `device.name_enc` in `login` and `recover` becomes optional, plus a "give your device a name" request after sign-in; then a new device does not sign in twice (currently the client signs in under a temporary name and disconnects it). Rules — appendix М (12 November).
- Tests before code: the П and Ф decisions with vectors, the server conformance suite for the new requests, core tests.
- Done when the tests are green in-process and against the container.

**Э3. Sync engine** (`sync/core`, without Tauri).

- Step 0: the engine rules are recorded in appendix В "Engine v1" (decisions Д1–Д72, Opus decisions В.9).
  The interface (the ports `LocalFs`, `Remote`, `StateStore`, `Clock`, `Random`) lives in `sync/core/src/engine/`,
  the simulation and the broken mini-engines in a separate crate `sync/sim`.
- Tests before code: the simulation from section 7 and the broken mini-engines it catches; separate tests for
  every row of the Р7 table, every rule of Р8 and Р9, every row of section 5.
- Tests before code, in addition to the above: consistency of header fields (a folder has no body and `size` is
  zero, a file has `body_id` and `content_hash`, and so on; the Э1 core checks only the shape of the fields, Ф14);
  streaming body opening writes to a temporary file next to the target and publishes it atomically only after
  checking all chunks, the length, the zeros and the hash, with memory bounded (Ф24); path syntax is checked by the core,
  system rules by the engine (Ф33, Р9); a fuzzing target for streaming body opening with a seed corpus
  of real bodies (the current `body_open` target barely reaches chunk parsing: 61 branches in 301 million
  runs, the result of the Э1 review).
- Code: reconciliation, conflicts, deletions, protections against mass loss, names, state.
- Done when the simulation passes tens of thousands of random scenarios and all the broken engines are still
  caught.

**Э4. Amby.**

- Tests before code: Rust tests of the glue with the vault (a sync write makes a `sync-overwrite` snapshot
  even with history turned off; a deletion goes to `.amby/trash/` in any deletion mode; an editor with an
  unsaved note gets a conflict; a sync write does not go back to the server;
  an unfinished journal delays sending); a test pinning the required behavior of a conflict copy with the same
  `amby-id` (Р7); interface behavior tests; a manual-check scenario on two computers.
- Additions from the Э3 review (appendix В.9): the app passes the engine rename hints (Д70); streaming body encryption in the core before syncing files larger than the memory limit (Д71; until then such files go to "not synced"); the app repeats passes with a growing pause (Д19). Tests for this are written before the code.
- Code: `src-tauri/src/sync/`, IPC and bindings, the `amby.sync` module, translations, a description of `.amby/sync/` in
  [vault-format.md](vault-format.md) and the rule about new files in `.amby/` (Р4).
- Moved over from Э3 (Opus review, 23.10): a "no access" variant of `FsError`, so that one inaccessible file
  does not stop the whole pass; a rename to a name that is invalid on this system does not upload the file
  a second time under the old path; the Д71 copy is visible in the conflict list; a test of reading a body with an
  `Interrupted` response; connection reuse in the server's test client (on macOS the runs hit
  TIME_WAIT ports and occasionally fail on the transport).
- **Gates before real notes** (the owner's decision, 22.10): one mutation-testing run of the engine
  (on Linux or with an accelerated test set: on this Mac about 12 hours) and a long simulation run.
  These checks were completed and confirmed by the owner on 27 October 2026; connecting is available for working vaults after a full external backup.
  **How the gates are run.** Both runs go in GitHub Actions ([sync-gates.yml](../.github/workflows/sync-gates.yml)),
  because on the Mac mutation testing alone takes about 12 hours. The launch is `git push origin HEAD:gates/run`
  (if the branch already exists, with `--force`): a run by button or on a schedule works only for a file on `main`, and
  `sync/` gets there only at release. Two runs do not go at the same time. Mutations: `cargo-mutants` 27.1.0 over all of
  `core/src` (trial run: 1501 mutants; now 64 parts), tests — core and simulation without `self_check.rs` (that one checks the mini-engine and does not
  touch the real one). Simulation: 64,000 random scenarios of the real engine (the `quick` and `folders` modes, 80 jobs
  of 4 processes, 100 scenarios per process in each mode) and separately a check that all the broken engines are still caught, from four starting seeds. The result is in the
  "Gate summary" job and the `gates-summary` artifact; the report: [docs/road/sync.md](road/sync.md).
  **What counts as passed.** Simulation: not a single failed seed and not a single "INFRA" (failed transport); a failed
  seed is analyzed and fixed, and after the fix the run is repeated in full. Mutations: every surviving mutant (in the
  table with file and line) is analyzed — either a test is added that is red on it (in a separate commit, before the
  fix), or the report records why the mutant is equivalent (with the reason); timeouts are checked to be
  loops and not a slow machine. `GATES_PASSED` in `src-tauri/src/sync/gate.rs` is changed to `true` only after
  the owner confirms both results; a run after which the engine code changed does not close the gates. The analysis of the trial run (221 surviving mutants, of which 52 equivalent with reasons, 3 unresolved) and the engine bug that was found are in [docs/road/sync.md](road/sync.md). A test that kills an infinite-loop mutant must come in the suite before the earlier tests (the `a0_…` files) and limit its own time, otherwise the mutant remains a timeout.
- Done when the tests are green and the manual scenario has passed on two real computers with the server in Docker.

**Э4б. Conflicts v2** (the owner's decisions, 23 October 2026, after the check on two computers). The exact rules, the vault format and the port for the interface — [appendix Г](#appendix-г-conflicts-v2); the К1 and К2 tests and the report — `sync-e4b-report.md` (local archive).

The check on two computers showed that conflict copies next to the note are confusing (who "won"
looks random), hide in settings and break the note's identity (`amby-id`). This stage changes that.

- **К1. Automatic merge** (strict, tests before code). If different places of one text note were edited on two
  devices, the engine merges the edits itself by the common base (a three-way merge by lines and Markdown
  blocks); a conflict arises only when the same place is edited. For merging, the device
  keeps the base — the content of the last shared version — in `.amby/sync/` (local, not synced).
  The merged result is written as an ordinary write with a history snapshot; bytes that no edit touched
  do not change. Binary files, canvases, Excalidraw and JSON are not merged automatically.
- **К2. The second version does not lie in the tree** (strict). Instead of a copy `Name.<id>-conflict.ext` next to the file,
  the second version is stored in `.amby/conflicts/` (a new `.amby/` file, **synced**, to be described in
  `docs/vault-format.md`), so that the conflict is visible and resolved from any device. Conflict markers
  (`<<<<<<<` and the like) are **never** written inside the file itself: the file stays clean and compatible with Obsidian.
  A conflict copy does not keep `amby-id` (decision 23.10); with К2 the question disappears, because there is no copy in the tree.
  Transition: old copies `*.<id>-conflict.*` continue to be read and are shown as conflicts.
- **К3. Note notices above the bottom bar** (lightweight). Above the note's bar (time, words,
  undo, lock) — a stack of notice cards and a lightbulb button above it that opens and hides them;
  the lightbulb is visible only when there are notices, with a count. **Opened by themselves**: a new conflict ("Another
  version of the note arrived from another computer → Compare") and anything where text is at risk (deleted while
  typing, could not be saved). A card disappears when the issue is resolved. **All** of a note's warnings
  move here from the top strips: a change outside Amby, a deletion, an `amby-id` error, an unsaved
  draft, sync notices.
- **К4. Comparison in Live mode** (lightweight, but with byte tests). Two versions side by side, rendered as a
  note; differing blocks are highlighted; each has a choice of "mine", "other", "both"; "Done" assembles the result.
  The result is assembled **from the source text of the chosen blocks**, not re-saved from the rendering: untouched
  bytes stay byte for byte. If a version does not pass the Live Preview tolerance, the comparison opens in the
  source view. For non-text files — choosing a version as a whole.
- Order: К1 and К2 (one strict tests session and one code session), in parallel К3 and К4 (lightweight).
- Done when two computers in the manual scenario of `docs/native-ui-smoke.md` merge edits in different
  places without a conflict, and a real conflict is resolved in the comparison from the notice.

**Э5. Completion.**

- Tests before code: restoring an earlier version of a file from the server; bringing back a deleted file from the server's
  versions.
- Moved over from Э4 (Opus review, 23.10): note properties per appendix З (replaces Ж: values in the header, presentation by groups, merge by notes) — **done and merged into `dev` on 5 November 2026**, awaiting manual checking on two computers; streaming body encryption (Д71) for files larger than the memory limit.
- Code: viewing and restoring versions, deleting a space from the server from the app, public
  documentation for a self-hosted server, the `docs/sync-format.md` contract, a security review of the whole stage.
- Publishing the Docker image and a release — only with the owner's separate permission.

## 7. How we verify

- **Deterministic engine simulation (Э3).** Several virtual devices with an in-memory file system and
  a server in the same process. Random actions by a given seed: edits, creations,
  renames, deletions, process crashes, network drops and delays, server restart and reset.
  A failed scenario is reproduced by its seed. After each scenario the following is checked:
  1. **Convergence:** when everything has settled, all devices have the same set of files with the same bytes.
  2. **Nothing lost:** every version the user ever saved on any device,
     is somewhere at the end: as a file, a conflict copy, a history snapshot, in the trash or in the versions on the server.
  3. **The server is blind:** there is no plaintext in its database and bodies.
  4. **Protections trigger:** mass changes, a server reset, an empty vault folder, a version rollback.

  Before the real engine is checked, the simulation must catch every broken mini-engine (section 6).

- **Cryptography:** vectors from the standards and from the Python reference, property tests, fuzzing (`cargo-fuzz`) of the parsing of everything that arrives
  from the server.
- **Server:** API integration tests, including commit races and retries with the same `commit_id`.
- **App:** native checks on two computers with the server in Docker: a conflict while an unsaved note
  is open, deletion on one device, a folder rename, a large file, a network drop
  in the middle of an upload, a disconnected disk, a server reset.
- The usual repository checks: `npm run build`, `npm run verify`, `npm run rust:test`, strict Clippy.

## 8. Risks

| Risk                                                   | What we do                                                                                                      |
| ------------------------------------------------------ | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| a bug in the sync engine leads to data loss            | the simulation (section 7), history snapshots, the trash, versions on the server, a stop on mass changes        |
| a conflict copy with the same `amby-id` blocks editing | a check before the UI, a fallback in Р7                                                                         |
| no system key store                                    | a password at every launch (Р5)                                                                                 |
| large vaults (tens of thousands of files)              | a fast check by size and time, BLAKE3, batched commits; measured at Э4                                          |
| frequent conflicts in `properties.json`                | values in the header, presentation by groups, merge per note (appendix З)                                       |
| tests are quietly tailored to the code                 | tests before code in a separate session, a red check, a ban on changing the tests, mutation testing (section 6) |
| the user loses both the password and the code          | mandatory saving of the code when sync is turned on, honest wording (О3)                                        |

## 9. What the owner can change

The defaults are chosen so that data stays safe. They can be changed without touching the
rest of the plan:

- how many days the server keeps previous versions — **30**;
- the maximum file size — **512 MiB**;
- the threshold for stopping on mass changes — **20 files or more and more than 10%**;
- whether to sync `.obsidian/` — **no**;
- the name in the UI — **«Синхронизация Amby»** (Amby Sync).

## Appendix А. Exact format v1

Here the bytes are fixed before the code (section 6, Э1, step 0). The Python reference (`sync/reference/`) and Rust
are written **from this appendix** and must match byte for byte. Decisions Р5 and Р6 remain the main ones:
the appendix only refines them. Each decision below is a separate row with a number `Ф…`. All of them were
verified by Opus on 20 October 2026 (the note "verified by Opus, 20.10"). If a decision changes,
the reference, the vectors and the tests change in one separate commit.

### А.0. Conventions

| №   | Decision                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Note                    |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------- |
| Ф1  | All integers in binary structures are big-endian: `epoch` is 4 bytes (u32), `version` is 8 bytes (u64). `‖` is concatenation. Strings in `info` and AAD are ASCII with no trailing zero. In JSON, bytes are written as lowercase hex. | verified by Opus, 20.10 |
| Ф2  | The identifiers `space_id`, `item_id`, `body_id` are 16 random bytes each. In binary structures they are raw bytes, in JSON they are 32 characters of lowercase hex.                                                                  | verified by Opus, 20.10 |
| Ф3  | The space key epoch number starts at 1 (always 1 in the first version). Epoch 0 is not allowed.                                                                                                                                       | verified by Opus, 20.10 |
| Ф4  | The format version of all binary envelopes is a single byte `0x01`. Any other value is an "unknown version" error (Р13), not an authenticity error.                                                                                   | verified by Opus, 20.10 |
| Ф5  | `SHA-256(auth_key)` is what the server stores instead of `auth_key` and `recovery_auth_key` (Р5). The server computes it; the core provides the function for the server conformance suite tests.                                      | verified by Opus, 20.10 |

### А.1. Password, Argon2id and HKDF

| №   | Decision                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Note                    |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------- |
| Ф6  | Before hashing, the password is normalized to Unicode NFKC and encoded as UTF-8, so that the same password typed on macOS (NFD) and on Windows gives the same key. The cryptography does not reject an empty password: the UI checks length and strength (Р5).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                      | verified by Opus, 20.10 |
| Ф7  | Argon2id version `0x13`, a 16-byte random salt, a 32-byte output, no `secret` and no `associated data`. Defaults (Р5): memory 65,536 KiB (64 MiB), passes `t = 3`, parallelism `p = 4`. The output is the master key.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | verified by Opus, 20.10 |
| Ф8  | The limits on the parameters the client accepts **from the server** (О8, protection against tampering): memory from 65,536 to 1,048,576 KiB, passes from 3 to 12, parallelism from 1 to 16. Below the limit the server could make password guessing easier, above it — hang the client. Anything outside the limits is an "invalid parameters" error before any computation starts. The lower limits equal the defaults on purpose: a malicious server could send weak parameters at sign-in, receive a cheaply computed `auth_key`, and guess the password faster. | verified by Opus, 20.10 |
| Ф9  | HKDF is RFC 5869 with SHA-256, the salt is **empty** (per the RFC this is `HashLen` zeros), the output is 32 bytes. The input material is the master key, the recovery code (32 bytes) or the VK.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | verified by Opus, 20.10 |
| Ф10 | `info` strings (ASCII): `amby-sync/auth/1` → `auth_key`; `amby-sync/kek/1` → `KEK`; `amby-sync/recovery-auth/1` → `recovery_auth_key`; `amby-sync/recovery-kek/1` → `recovery_KEK`; `amby-sync/header-key/1` → the header key from the VK; `amby-sync/body/1` ‖ `body_id` (16 raw bytes) → the body key from the VK.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | verified by Opus, 20.10 |

### А.2. Key wrappers for AK and VK

A wrapper is XChaCha20-Poly1305 with a random 24-byte nonce. The layout is the same for AK and VK:

```text
wrapper = 0x01 ‖ nonce (24) ‖ ciphertext (32) ‖ tag (16)      73 bytes in total
```

| №   | Decision                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | Note                    |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ----------------------- |
| Ф11 | AK is wrapped with the key `KEK` (by password) or `recovery_KEK` (by code). The AAD of the AK wrapper: `amby-sync/wrap-ak/1`. The keys differ, so the wrappers by password and by code do not substitute for each other. | verified by Opus, 20.10 |
| Ф12 | VK is wrapped with the key AK. The AAD of the VK wrapper: `amby-sync/wrap-vk/1` ‖ `space_id` (16) ‖ `epoch` (4). The server cannot pass off the VK of one space or epoch as the VK of another.                           | verified by Opus, 20.10 |
| Ф13 | The order of the wrapper fields: version, nonce, ciphertext with the tag at the end (as the library returns it). The only plaintext parts are the version and the nonce.                                                 | verified by Opus, 20.10 |

### А.3. Header

| №   | Decision                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Note                    |
| --- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------- |
| Ф14 | JSON fields: `format` (integer, `1`), `version` (integer ≥ 0, equal to the record's version number on the server), `kind` (`"file"` or `"folder"`), `path` (a string in NFC, Р9), `size` (integer ≥ 0), `mtime_ms` (integer, may be negative), `content_hash` (64 characters of lowercase hex of the BLAKE3 of the content, or `null` for a folder), `body_id` (32 characters of lowercase hex or `null` for a folder), `deleted` (`true`/`false`). The other keys are unknown fields of newer versions (Р13): they are preserved as they are, the core gives them no meaning.            | verified by Opus, 20.10 |
| Ф15 | **Canonical JSON.** UTF-8 encoding without a BOM. No spaces or line breaks: the separators are `,` and `:`. Object keys (at all levels) are sorted by the bytes of their UTF-8 (that is, by code points). Strings are escaped minimally: `\"`, `\\`, control characters U+0000–U+001F as `\b \t \n \f \r`, the rest as `\u00XX` in lowercase hex; everything else, including non-ASCII and U+007F, is written as is. Numbers are integers only, in decimal notation with no plus sign, no leading zeros and no exponent; fractional and exponential numbers are rejected.                 | verified by Opus, 20.10 |
| Ф16 | A known key among the unknown fields on writing is a "reserved key" error. A duplicate key in any JSON object on parsing is a `Malformed` error: our writer never creates duplicates, and different parsers handle them differently (some take the last, others fail), so strictness removes the discrepancy.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | verified by Opus, 20.10 |
| Ф17 | **Padding to 256 bytes.** Zero bytes are appended to the bytes of the canonical JSON until the length is a multiple of 256. If the length is already a multiple of 256, nothing is added. The JSON does not end with a zero, so on parsing all trailing zeros are stripped unambiguously. A length that is not a multiple of 256 (or is zero) is a "wrong padding" error; reading allows only zeros at the end, anything else is a parse error.                                                                                                                                           | verified by Opus, 20.10 |
| Ф18 | **Header envelope**: `0x01` ‖ `epoch` (4) ‖ nonce (24) ‖ ciphertext (the length of the padded JSON) ‖ tag (16). The key is `HKDF(VK, "amby-sync/header-key/1")`. AAD: `amby-sync/header/1` ‖ `space_id` (16) ‖ `item_id` (16) ‖ `epoch` (4) ‖ `version` (8); 62 bytes in total. The `epoch` in the envelope is in the clear so that the right VK can be chosen, and it must match the VK's epoch. Any mismatch between the envelope's epoch and the VK's epoch, including 0, is `EpochMismatch`. Reading the epoch from an envelope (`header_envelope_epoch`) for epoch 0 is `Malformed`. | verified by Opus, 20.10 |
| Ф19 | The `version` inside the JSON must match the version from the AAD, otherwise "versions do not match" (Р6). A `format` newer than `1` is "format is newer" (Р13): the record is not applied and not rewritten. A `path` not in NFC is an error both on writing and on reading.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | verified by Opus, 20.10 |
| Ф33 | **Path syntax** (protection against writing outside the vault if a device sends a bad path). `seal_header`, `open_header`, `canonical_header_json` and `parse_header_json` reject a path with the error `InvalidPath` if it: is empty; starts or ends with `/`; contains an empty segment (`a//b`); contains a segment `.` or `..`; contains `\`; contains characters U+0000–U+001F or U+007F; is longer than 4096 bytes in UTF-8. This check runs before the NFC check (`PathNotNfc`). The rules of a specific system (`:`, `CON`, case, and so on) remain with the engine (Р9).         | verified by Opus, 20.10 |

### А.3а. Device and space names

Only the owner reads the name: the server stores the bytes as they are (О1).

| №   | Decision                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              | Note                    |
| --- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ----------------------- |
| Ф34 | **Device and space names.** The name key is `HKDF(AK, "amby-sync/name-key/1")` (Ф9, empty salt, 32 bytes): AK itself is not used directly as a cipher. Envelope: `0x01` ‖ nonce (24) ‖ ciphertext (64) ‖ tag (16), always **105 bytes**. The plaintext is the name in UTF-8 (at most 64 bytes), padded with zeros to 64 bytes; on reading, trailing zeros are stripped, and a name with a zero inside is not allowed. The AAD for a device is `amby-sync/device-name/1`, for a space — `amby-sync/space-name/1` ‖ `space_id` (16). Randomness: one nonce (24 bytes). The server requires `name_enc` to be exactly 105 bytes, otherwise `400 bad_request` (П12, П20). **Done in Э4 (the connection screen), not in Э3:** the functions `seal_device_name`, `open_device_name`, `seal_space_name`, `open_space_name` are in `sync/core/src/crypto/name.rs`; the vectors are the `names` section in `vectors/format-v1.json`, produced by the independent reference (`sync/reference`); the conformance suite `sync/conformance` still encrypts names with its own code and does not depend on the core. | verified by Opus, 20.10 |

### А.4. Body (STREAM)

The STREAM mode is like `aead::stream::EncryptorBE32` from RustCrypto on top of XChaCha20-Poly1305.

```text
body          = preamble ‖ chunk 0 ‖ … ‖ last chunk
preamble      = "AMBYBODY" (8) ‖ 0x01 ‖ epoch (4) ‖ nonce prefix (19)         32 bytes in total
nonce of chunk i = prefix (19) ‖ i (4, big-endian) ‖ flag (1)                 flag 0x01 only on the last chunk
```

| №   | Decision                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             | Note                    |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ | ----------------------- |
| Ф20 | The body key is `HKDF(VK, "amby-sync/body/1" ‖ body_id)`. A new `body_id` on every upload (Р6): a key is never used for two different bodies.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | verified by Opus, 20.10 |
| Ф21 | The plaintext of a chunk is exactly 65,536 bytes, except the last one (0–65,536 bytes). The ciphertext of a chunk is 16 bytes longer. The number of chunks is `max(1, ⌈P / 65 536⌉)`, where `P` is the length of the padded content (Ф22). An empty file has one last chunk consisting of a single tag (16 bytes). There is never an extra empty chunk after a full last one.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                        | verified by Opus, 20.10 |
| Ф22 | The padded content is the file content ‖ zeros up to the length `P = padme(size)`. `size` and `content_hash` are taken from the already verified header.
