The path keeps the record with the smaller `item_id`, identically on all devices; the second record moves to a conflict-copy name (Д46) and, like any copy, ends up in the conflict list (Д47) — the latter is finished in Э4. **Clarification (seed 1207, 24.10):** the rule works in both directions. If a record with the _smaller_ `item_id` arrives at a path by a rename, and the device has a file of a record with the _larger_ `item_id` at that path, this file first moves to a copy name (a conflict set), its move is sent to the server in the same pass, and the record with the smaller id is resolved anew in the next pass, when the path is free. While the losing file lies at the path, the winner's record is not placed at the path: otherwise the loser would look modified, and its move would not be sent. Without this the devices decided in opposite ways, and the rename waited forever (`PathBusy`). **For Opus review.** | verified by Opus, 23.10 |
| Д71 | **File size limit.** `EngineConfig.max_file_bytes` — the largest file that this device reads and encrypts entirely in memory. A file larger than the limit (its own or an incoming one) is neither read nor written, and goes to "not synchronized" with the reason `TooBigForMemory`. The limit will be lifted by streaming body encryption in the core (Э4).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                     | verified by Opus, 21.10 |
| Д72 | **A real reset.** A reset server knows neither the user nor the space, and the user is not created anew. The device sees a different `instance_id` (`ServerReset`) or gets `401` (`AuthRequired`), stops, and deletes, renames, or overwrites nothing locally; the state does not change.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                          | verified by Opus, 21.10 |

## Appendix Г. Conflicts v2

Here the rules of stage Э4б (К1 — merge, К2 — conflict store) are fixed before the code (section 6, Э4б, step 0, strict order). The tests (`sync/core/tests/engine_merge*.rs`, `sync/core/tests/engine_conflict_*.rs`, `sync/sim`, `src-tauri/src/sync/*_tests.rs`) and the code are written **according to this appendix**. It refines Р4, Р7, Р10, and appendix В; what it **replaces** is listed in Г.4. Each decision is a separate item `Г…`; all were verified by Opus on October 23, 2026. Open questions are in Г.6. The port rules for the К3/К4 interface are in Г.3.

### Г.0. Terms

- **Г1. Terms.** The _local_ version is the content of the file on disk. The _server_ version is the latest version of the record in the feed. The _base_ is the content of the last common version (Д1), not just its hash. The _second version_ is the one of the two that did not take the path. A _conflict set_ is the folder `.amby/conflicts/<id>/` (Г21). A _place_ is the unit within which two edits count as a conflict (Г11). — _verified by Opus, 23.10_
- **Г2. Who takes the path — as before.** Д29, Д31, Д43: the server version stays at the path, the local one becomes the second. Д56, Д57 (`resync`): the local one stays at the path, the server one becomes the second. The file at the path is the same on all devices (convergence, section 7). Merging changes nothing here: it simply makes it so that no second version remains. — _verified by Opus, 23.10_
- **Г3. Two modes, one decision Д16.** Table В.3 and the `decide` function do not change: rows Д29 and Д31 still say "both sides changed the content". A merge and a conflict set are an **action** of step 7 (applying), not a new table row. This way the mass-change safeguard (Д54) counts a merged file as a replacement, as before. — _verified by Opus, 23.10_

### Г.1. Merge (К1)

- **Г4. Where merging happens.** Only in rows Д29 and Д31 (both sides edited the content of one record), at step 7. Not in Д43 (both created the file: there is no common version), not in Д56/Д57 (the state is erased, there is no base), not in Д30, Д33, Д35 (the content was changed by one side: its version is taken, as before), not in Д32, Д36–Д39 (deletion). — _verified by Opus, 23.10_
- **Г5. Which files.** A file is merged only if **all** conditions hold: the path ends in `.md` (case-insensitive), the path is not inside `.amby/`; the base, local, and server versions are valid UTF-8 without a `0x00` byte; each is no larger than `MERGE_MAX_BYTES` = 1 MiB. Otherwise there is no merge, straight to a conflict set (Г21). Canvases, Excalidraw, JSON, database manifests, and attachments are never merged automatically (Р7); the only exception is `.amby/properties.json` (appendix Ж). — _verified by Opus, 23.10_
- **Г6. Base: what and where.** The bytes of the last common version of the file, **only** for files that qualify under Г5 (a `.md` path, UTF-8 without `0x00`, no larger than 1 MiB). Stored locally in `.amby/sync/bases/`, through the `StateStore` port (`base_put`, `base_get`, `base_prune`, Г7). The `sync/` folder is not synchronized, so the base never leaves the device. The key is `(item_id, content hash)`: a base always corresponds to a specific version. It lies in the open, like the notes themselves (the device is trusted; the server does not see it). The cost is a copy of every synchronized note up to 1 MiB; it is recoverable (if lost, there will still be a conflict, not a merge) and in Р10 it does not count as user data. — _verified by Opus, 23.10_
- **Г7. The base port (`StateStore`).** `base_put(item, hash, bytes)`: writes atomically (temporary file, rename) and durably before returning; a repeat is harmless. `base_get(item, hash)`: returns the bytes **only if** the BLAKE3 of the bytes equals `hash`; no file, damaged, someone else's — `Ok(None)`, not an error. `base_prune(keep)`: deletes all bases except the listed pairs. — _verified by Opus, 23.10_
- **Г8. When the base is updated.** In the four cases of Д1 and only in them: (a) the file is written to disk from the server version — base = the written bytes; (b) our commit is accepted — base = the sent bytes; (c) the local and server versions match and are linked (Д15) — base = the file bytes (the engine read them for the hash or reads them again); (d) a deletion is confirmed — the base is deleted. A file that does not qualify under Г5 gets no base, and the record's previous base is deleted. A rename does not change the base (same hash). — _verified by Opus, 23.10_
- **Г9. Order of writing the base and the state.** The base is written **before** the state transaction that references it (Д4: the world is ahead of the state). For (b) — before the commit is sent (keyed by hash, so an unaccepted commit spoils nothing). At the end of each pass `base_prune` keeps only the `(item_id, content_hash)` pairs from the state records and from `PendingCommit.after`. — _verified by Opus, 23.10_
- **Г10. No base — a conflict, not a merge.** There will be no merge and there will be a conflict set (Г21) if: `base_get` returned `None` (no base, damaged, hash did not match); the state is lost or the records were reset by a full reconciliation (Д8), a feed gap (Д58), `resync` (Д56, Д57); the file does not qualify under Г5. A merge "from two versions without a base" never happens: without a base any difference is a conflict. — _verified by Opus, 23.10_
- **Г11. "One place".** The merge works **by lines**, with these units: (1) an ordinary line — itself; (2) a **fenced code block** (lines from the opening ` ``` ` or `~~~` to the closing one of the same character; an unclosed one runs to the end of the file) — as a whole; (3) a **table** (consecutive lines starting with `|` after optional spaces) — as a whole; (4) a **formula block** from `$$` to `$$` — as a whole; (5) a **YAML header** (the first line of the file is `---`, and further on there is a closing line `---` or `...`; otherwise there is no header, and a `---` later in the text is an ordinary line) — by top-level **keys**: a key is its line (`name: …`) together with the following lines that start with a space, a tab, or `- `, and empty lines, up to the next key; the `---` lines are separate places. Units are counted in the **base**. An edit touching at least one line of a unit expands to the whole unit; an insertion strictly inside a unit (not at its boundary) does too. — _verified by Opus, 23.10_
- **Г12. Algorithm (three-way merge, line-based `diff3`).** (1) A line is the bytes up to and including `\n`; the last line of the file may lack `\n`. `\r` is part of the line. Nothing is normalized. (2) The first three bytes `EF BB BF` (BOM) at the start of a version are separated from the text before the merge and put back after it (Г14). (3) Base→local and base→server: the smallest number of changed lines (the longest common subsequence of whole lines, byte-wise comparison); the result is _edits_: a range of base lines `[s, e)` (empty for an insertion) and a replacement (a list of lines). (4) The ranges are expanded to places (Г11). (5) A local edit and a server edit **conflict** if their ranges **overlap**: two non-empty ranges share a base line (`a.s < b.e` and `b.s < a.e`); an insertion at position `p` overlaps a non-empty range `[s, e)` only if `s < p < e` (strictly inside); two insertions overlap only if the position is the same. Ranges that merely **touch** (`a.e = b.s`) do not conflict: edits of adjacent lines and an insertion at the boundary of another edit merge, and the order of the result is unambiguous: by the start of the range, and with an equal start the insertion goes before the non-empty edit. The exception is **identical** edits (the same range and the same replacement): one is taken. Insertions at the same position with different text conflict, insertions at different positions (with a base line between them) do not. A **line without a trailing `\n`** (the last line of a version) closes the file: if one side's replacement ends with such a line, then the other side's insertion right after it (at the end) is a conflict, otherwise two lines would be glued into one. This way the outcome does not depend on which of the equivalent alignments `diff` chose (for example, deleting a paragraph together with the blank line above or below). (6) If there are no conflicts, the result is the base with all edits applied in order; otherwise the result is a whole-file conflict (no partial merge: "all or nothing"), and the list of regions is the unions of overlapping edits in base lines. (7) The checks of Г13. Time: if, after discarding the common beginning and end, the product of the number of base lines and version lines exceeds 25,000,000, the merge does not attempt and answers "too complex" (a conflict). — _verified by Opus, 23.10_
- **Г13. Shortcuts and result checks.** First the suitability of the content is checked (Г5), then: if local equals server — the result is it; if local equals the base — the server one; if server equals the base — the local one (property Г15 (б) holds for any texts). Only after that come the result checks. The merge answers with a conflict, not a clean result, if: (a) a merge is needed (both sides differ from the base and from each other), and either side changed the `amby-id` line in the header (we do not silently change identity); (b) the resulting header has a repeated top-level key that was not in the base (both sides added the same key in different places). Beyond that the merge checks nothing about meaning: it guarantees that **no edit is lost and no untouched byte is changed**, not that the result makes sense (Г.6, question 3). — _verified by Opus, 23.10_
- **Г14. Untouched bytes and BOM.** The result is the unchanged lines of the base **byte for byte** plus the replacements of the edits **byte for byte**. Line endings, trailing spaces, Unicode character encoding (NFC/NFD), and blank lines are preserved as they are. If a side edited only a line ending, that is an edit of that line. BOM: if it is the same in local and server, it stays; otherwise the one that differs from the base is taken (with a boolean value the choice is always unambiguous); an empty file has no BOM. A mix of `\n` and `\r\n` in a file remains a mix. — _verified by Opus, 23.10_
- **Г15. Properties that must hold.** (а) `merge(x, x, x) = x`. (б) If a side did not change: `merge(b, a, b) = a` and `merge(b, b, a) = a` byte for byte. (в) If both made the same edit: `merge(b, a, a) = a`. (г) Symmetry: `merge(b, l, r)` and `merge(b, r, l)` are both clean with the same bytes or both conflicts. (д) A clean result contains every line that either side added or replaced, and contains no line that either side deleted and the other did not restore (in property tests the lines are unique, so the alignment is unambiguous). (е) The result consists only of lines of the base, local, and server versions: the merge **never** writes conflict markers (`<<<<<<<`, `=======`, `>>>>>>>`). — _verified by Opus, 23.10_
- **Г16. Writing the result.** A clean result is written by an ordinary file write (`StagedWrite::publish(Some(SyncOverwrite))`): the previous local content is saved as a history snapshot (Д53), even when history is off. If the result equals the local one (the server edits are already in it) — the file is not written; if the result equals the server version (the local edits are already in it) — the file is replaced by it, as in Д25, with a snapshot, and nothing goes to the server. In the state the record gets the **server** version (path, hash, version number), and the file stays "modified locally", so the same pass sends the result at step 8 as an edit Д22 with `base_version` = the server version. Nothing special is needed on the server for a new version: **the merged result goes as a new version** by the ordinary path. The base (Г8) until it is sent is the server version, and after the commit is accepted — the merged bytes. — _verified by Opus, 23.10_
- **Г17. Report.** `CycleReport.merged_paths` — the (server) paths where edits were merged in this pass. This stage adds no messages for the user; the field is needed by the tests and К3. — _verified by Opus, 23.10_
- **Г17а. The port event about a merge.** When a pass has `merged_paths`, the session reports these paths to the app once per pass (`SessionHooks::merged_after_edits`), the app sends the `sync-merged` event with the paths in the vault, and the interface port provides `onMerged(listener(paths))`. The interface (К3) shows a quiet card "Edits from two computers were merged" above the note's banner (it does not open by itself) with a link to the previous version in history (the `sync-overwrite` snapshot, Г16); in Г there is only the report field and this event. Without a merge there is no event. — _verified by Opus, 23.10_
- **Г18. Failures.** The merge is deterministic from its three inputs, so a repeat after a crash at any point gives the same result; only the file and `PutItem` are written (Д4). If the local file changed after the decision, the action is not performed and is repeated in the next pass (Д17, С9). A base read error is treated as its absence (Г10). — _verified by Opus, 23.10_
- **Г19. Paths inside `.amby/conflicts/`.** There are no merges or sets there (Г5, Г22): when two records diverge at such a path, the previous copy `Name.<id>-conflict.ext` next to it applies (Д46). In practice this does not happen: the set name is computed from the content. — _verified by Opus, 23.10_

### Г.2. Conflict store (К2)

- **Г20. Instead of a copy alongside.** Everywhere that Д29, Д31, Д43, Д49, Д51, Д56, Д57, Д71 put a copy `Name.<id>-conflict.ext` next to the file, the second version now lies in a conflict set. A copy is no longer created in the notes tree. Conflict markers are never written inside a file (Г15 (е)). — _verified by Opus, 23.10_
- **Г21. Set format.** The folder `.amby/conflicts/<id>/` (`<id>` is 12 lowercase hex characters):
  - `other<ext>` — the bytes of the second version "as is" (for `Заметка.md` (Note.md) — `other.md`; `<ext>` is the original's extension with the dot, if it consists of 1–8 characters `A–Z a–z 0–9`, otherwise empty). Any file format.
  - `base<ext>` — the bytes of the base (Г6), **only** for a `bothEdited` conflict that had a base.
  - `meta.json` — compact JSON in UTF-8, keys in this order, without spaces and without a trailing newline: `{"v":1,"id":…,"kind":"bothEdited"|"bothCreated","item_id":<32 hex>,"path":<original path in NFC>,"other":<other file name>,"other_hash":<BLAKE3 hex>,"other_size":<number>,"base":<name or null>,"base_hash":<hex or null>}`.
    There is **no** time, device name, or anything that depends on the device in the set: two devices that create the same set write the same bytes and link without a conflict (Д15). — _verified by Opus, 23.10_
- **Г22. Set identifier.** `id` — the first 6 bytes of `BLAKE3("amby-sync/conflict/2" ‖ item_id ‖ hash of the second version (the one going into `other`) ‖ server version number (8 bytes BE) ‖ base hash (32 bytes; 32 zeros if there is no base))` and, if the attempt is not zero, 4 bytes BE of the attempt number, as lowercase hex. The base is part of the hash so that two devices with different bases do not write different `meta.json` under the same `id`. The occupancy rules (Д46) are the same: occupied by a set with the same `other` — the set already exists; occupied by another — the next attempt; the same set already on the server — it will arrive by itself. The previous formula (`…/conflict/1`) remains for previous copies and for Г19. — _verified by Opus, 23.10_
- **Г23. Creation order and integrity.** First `other`, then `base`, then `meta.json`. A set **exists** only when `meta.json` is present and parses, and the `other` file is present whose BLAKE3 equals `other_hash` (and, if specified, the base with `base_hash`). An incomplete set is not shown and interferes with nothing; a repeat of the same pass completes it (the names are the same). All files of a set created in one pass go in one commit when there are no more than `max_commit_items` of them. Cleaning up incomplete sets is an open question (Г.6, question 5). **Clarification:** a set whose second version (`other`) or base was changed after `meta.json` was written **stays open and visible** in the list: it does not disappear and is not considered incomplete. That the file has changed is shown by the stale-version check during comparison and resolution (К4); the choice stays with the person. — _verified by Opus, 23.10; clarification verified by Opus, 24.10_
- **Г24. What is synchronized.** The whole of `.amby/conflicts/`: yes. This is an addition to Р4 and Д11: `is_excluded_path` does not exclude `.amby/conflicts/**`. The set files are ordinary records (Д45); on another device they arrive as a creation (Д41), and there the set appears in the list. The table in Р4 and `docs/vault-format.md` are updated; the `rules_doc_tests` test keeps them together. — _verified by Opus, 23.10_
- **Г25. The conflict list is files.** The list of open conflicts on any device is assembled from the existing sets (Г23) and from the previous copies (Г29); the record in the state (`SyncState.conflicts`) remains and means "created on this device" (`createdHere`), `ConflictRecord.copy_path` = the path of the `other` file. A record whose set has disappeared is removed from the state (as now in `open_conflicts`). — _verified by Opus, 23.10_
- **Г26. How a conflict is cleared from any device.** Resolution (Г33) does the following in order: (1) writes the result to the original file by an ordinary write with a history snapshot: `Current` — writes nothing; `Other` — the bytes of `other`; `Assembled(text)` — the passed text as is; (2) deletes the set files into the vault trash (`.amby/trash/`): the second version stays recoverable; (3) removes the record from the state. After that ordinary synchronization works: the file edit goes as Д22, the set deletion as Д24, on the other devices the file arrives as Д25, the set as Д28 (into their trash), and the conflict disappears from their lists. A crash between (1) and (2) is safe: repeating the resolution gives the same result. Two devices that resolve differently at the same time get an ordinary file conflict (through Г4 and Г20). — _verified by Opus, 23.10_
- **Г27. Link to the file.** By `item_id`: the original's path is taken from the state by record, and if it is absent, from `path` in `meta.json`. Renaming the original does not break the link. If the original is not on disk: `Current` — error `OriginalMissing`, nothing changes; `Other` and `Assembled` create the file at the last known path if it is free, otherwise — error `OriginalMissing`. — _verified by Opus, 23.10_
- **Г28. Non-text files.** If a file does not qualify under Г5 (including too large and not UTF-8), `other` lies in the set as is, and there is no `base`. The port returns `text = false`; resolution is only `Current` or `Other` as a whole; `Assembled` is an error `NotText`. — _verified by Opus, 23.10_
- **Г29. Previous copies (transition without migration).** Files `Name.<12 hex>-conflict.ext` in the user's tree are not touched and not moved. The port shows them as it does now (`open_conflicts`: the copy and the original in place; a copy without its own `amby-id`, or a record in the state), with `source = legacyCopy`, `text` by the same rules, without a base. Resolution: `Current` — the copy to the trash; `Other` — the copy replaces the original with a history snapshot, the copy to the trash; `Assembled` — the text is written to the original, the copy to the trash. The previous "Keep both" is not part of the new port (the copy stays an ordinary file); the `resolve_conflict` command with the old `ConflictChoice` remains until К3. The index rule "a copy does not hold an `amby-id`" (23.10) is kept. — _verified by Opus, 23.10_
- **Г30. Note identity.** The set files lie in `.amby/`, and the note index does not read them: there is no `amby-id` question for the second version anymore. The content of `other` with the original's `amby-id` is written back only with `Other`/`Assembled`, like any text. — _verified by Opus, 23.10_

### Г.3. Conflict store port for the interface (К3, К4)

The interface (`src/`) talks to the `SyncPort` port (`src/lib/sync/sync-port.ts`) and does not know where the second version lives. Storage changes **behind the port**: on the Rust side it is the `ConflictStore` trait in `src-tauri/src/sync/conflict_store.rs`.

- **Г31. Three operations.** `list()`, `read(id)`, `resolve(id, resolution)`. All of them work on files, with no network and no pass (resolution starts a pass after itself, as it does now).
- **Г32. `list`.** Each entry: `id`, `kind` (`bothEdited`/`bothCreated`), `path` (the current path of the original), `otherPath` (where the second version lives; informational), `createdMs` (the modification time of `meta.json` or of the copy; for display only), `source` (`bundle`/`legacyCopy`), `text` (whether a line-by-line comparison is possible: Г28), `hasBase`, `createdHere`. Order is by `createdMs`, then by `id`. The `status.conflicts` field in `VaultSyncStatus` (`ConflictView`) gets the same fields **by addition**: the previous ones (`id`, `kind`, `originalPath`, `copyPath`, `createdMs`) stay. **Clarification:** in TypeScript the new fields (`source`, `text`, `hasBase`, `createdHere`) are optional, so that the old five-field objects remain a valid `ConflictView`; the interface reads a missing field as "unknown". — _clarification checked by Opus, 24.10_
- **Г33. `read` and `resolve`.** `read(id)` returns `current` (the file at the path right now), `other`, and `base` (if present): for each version — its size and hash, and for `text = true` also the **text** (`String`, exactly those bytes, no normalization; the BOM as the character U+FEFF at the start, `\r\n` preserved). For `text = false` no bytes go through IPC. `resolve(id, resolution)`, where `resolution` is one of: `{kind:"current"}` ("mine" in the sense of "the file as it is now"), `{kind:"other"}` ("the other"), `{kind:"assembled", text}` ("assembled text": the interface builds it from the **source text** of the chosen blocks of both versions; the port writes **exactly these bytes** as UTF-8 to the file and normalizes nothing and appends nothing, including the line break at the end).
- **Г34. "Mine" and "the other" in the interface.** The port is neutral: `current` is the file in the tree, `other` is the second version. The interface picks the labels by `createdHere`: if the set was created on this device, `other` is **your** displaced edit and `current` is the one that arrived from the other computer; if not, the reverse. The outcome is the same on every device, because `current` is the same file everywhere (Г2).
- **Г35. Port errors.** `not_found` (no such `id`), `incomplete` (the set is incomplete, Г23), `original_missing` (Г27), `not_text` (Г28), `storage_error`. These are machine codes; the interface translates them itself (like `SyncPortError`).
- **Г36. Names at the IPC boundary — as in the `sync/e4b-ui` branch (checked 23.10).** The К3/К4 interface already works with `readConflictVersions(id)` → `ConflictVersions { original, copy }` (text or `null` when it cannot be shown), `resolveConflictWithText(id, text)` (commands `sync_read_conflict_versions`, `sync_resolve_conflict_with_text`) and the existing `resolveConflict(id, choice)`; the list arrives in `VaultSyncStatus.conflicts` and via the `sync-status-changed` event. The К2 implementation **introduces no second names**, but swaps the storage behind them: `original` is the text of `current`, `copy` is the text of `other` (`null` when `text = false`, Г28); `resolveConflictWithText` is `Resolution::Assembled`; `resolveConflict`: `KeepOriginal` → `Current`, `KeepCopy` → `Other`, `KeepBoth` — the transitional behavior of Г29. The `ConflictStore` trait (Г31) is the internal Rust layer behind these commands; its type with the versions is called `ConflictDetails`, so as not to clash with the IPC type. The merge event is Г17а (`onMerged`). — _checked by Opus, 23.10_

### Г.6. Open questions for Opus

1. **A base on the server instead of a base on disk.** The body of the previous version lies on the server for 30 days (П35) and is downloaded by `body_id`. Then the base on the device is not always needed: no local base → download the previous version. This gives merging even after `.amby/sync/` is lost, but ties merging to the network and to the retention period. A base on disk is chosen here (the owner's decision, К1); a server base is a possible addition later.
2. **The "place" unit (Г11) and neighboring lines (Г12 item 5).** Lines are chosen (+ whole code blocks, tables, formulas, header keys); edits of neighboring lines merge (only an overlap conflicts). Stricter would be "touching edits conflict" (as in git), but then the outcome depends on the choice of an equivalent `diff` alignment (deleting a paragraph against editing its neighbor), and two different items of one list would conflict. The alternative is the paragraph as the unit (simpler to explain, but coarser).
3. **The meaning of the result (Г13).** A merge guarantees "nothing is lost, the untouched is unchanged", but not the meaning (for example, two edits, each correct on its own, contradict each other together). The only protection is the history snapshot (Г16) and the fact that the user sees the result. Is that enough, or should a "the edits of two computers were combined" notice (`merged_paths`) be shown for a while at first?
4. **"All or nothing" (Г12 item 6).** If a file has both mergeable and conflicting places, the whole file goes into a set, and the block-by-block comparison is done by the interface (К4). The alternative — merge what is mergeable and leave the conflicting places in the set — is more complex and more dangerous.
5. **Incomplete sets (Г23).** They are harmless, but nobody cleans them up. I propose not to remove anything automatically in this stage and to show them in the log; cleanup comes later, after there is data on whether they occur.
6. **Paths in `.amby/conflicts/` (Г19).** The previous copy alongside stays there, to avoid getting a set of a set. The alternative — forbid conflicts in this folder, accepting the server version and saving the local one to history; I rejected it because it is a data loss, even if in an unlikely case.
7. **Port names.** `Current`/`Other` instead of "mine"/"the other" (Г34): the labels depend on which device the set was created on, and the interface decides them itself.

## Appendix Е. Plans and limits

Status: **checked by Opus 24.10, decisions made; no code and no tests.** Supplements appendices Б (protocol)
and В (engine); decisions Р1–Р14, П1–П42 and Д1–Д72 stay in force, except for the rows that Е.7 explicitly changes (П42, П35, П36).
The letter "Д" is skipped in the appendix name, so as not to confuse it with the engine decisions `Д…`. Numbering continues:
protocol and server — **П50…** (П43–П49 are left for the proxy requests from Э4, the row about Ф35), engine — **Д73…**,
application — **У1…**. Order of work is section 6: this entry → Opus check → a separate session writes red tests
→ another session writes the code with the tests frozen.

The decisions of the owner and the coordinator that everything is built on:

1. **Plans are a server setting, not application code.** A self-hosted server is, by default, without per-user limits.
2. **A limit never deletes anything — not on the device, not on the server, not on other devices.** A file that is too
   large stays only on this device and is listed under "not synced"; its copies on other
   devices stay. A full quota stops **sending**, while receiving continues, and the person sees
   a notice.
3. **A plan is a named set of limits**; the administrator assigns it to a user with a command. There is no payment yet.
4. The owner's sample plans for the hosted "Amby Server" (like Obsidian Sync): "Standard" — 1 vault,
   1 GB, file up to 20 MB, history 30 days; "Plus" — 10 vaults, 10 GB, file up to 200 MB, history 365 days (the "Standard" file limit is 20 MB by the owner's decision). Units are **decimal** everywhere (1 GB = 1,000,000,000 bytes), as in Finder. This is
   an example of a settings file (Е.1), not code constants. The word "vault" in the interface is the protocol's "space".

### Е.0. What exists now (per the code, 24.10)

- The server knows three shared limits: `AMBY_SYNC_MAX_BLOB_BYTES` (the body after encryption, П8), `AMBY_SYNC_QUOTA_BYTES_PER_USER`
  (П42, off by default) and `AMBY_SYNC_RETAIN_VERSION_DAYS` (П35, П36). All are in `sync/server/src/config.rs`.
- The quota is currently checked only in `POST …/blobs` (`http/blobs.rs`) and counts **all** rows of the user's `blobs` table:
  latest versions, earlier ones, those deleted before cleanup, and unfinished ones. Because of this, deleting a file **does not free
  the space for 30 days** (the body stays in the history) — a person at the limit gets stuck. This is the main defect that П55–П56 fix.
- There is no account in the API: `GET /v1/account` does not exist; `users` is a name, a password, keys (`db.rs`, `SCHEMA_V1`);
  `PRAGMA user_version` = 1; migrations are plain SQL without parameters. A space cannot be deleted (Б.10-12, Э5).
- Engine: `UnsyncedReason::TooLarge` (the body is larger than `info.max_blob_bytes`) and `TooBigForMemory` (Д71) already exist, the
  "not synced" list is recomputed every pass (`scan.rs`, `SetUnsynced`). Any `RemoteError::Rejected` from
  `blob_start` currently aborts the pass as an unknown error. The file size is checked before sending by `sealed_body_len`.
- Application: `src-tauri/src/sync/space.rs` (`create_space`, `open_space`, `list_spaces`), the settings section
  `src/components/workspace/sync/sync-settings.tsx`.

### Е.1. Plans on the server

| No. | Decision                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | Note                   |
| --- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------- |
| П50 | **A plan** is a name `[a-z0-9_-]{1,32}` and five limits, each optional: `max_spaces` (number of spaces, integer ≥ 1), `max_total_bytes` (sum of encrypted bytes, П55), `max_file_bytes` (the largest body after encryption, as in П8, ≥ 1), `history_days` (days of retention for earlier and deleted versions, ≥ 1), `max_history_bytes` (history ceiling, П57-д; absent — no ceiling, a self-hosted server does not change). The plan's promise: "history of N days, but no more than M". An optional `title` field is a label for people. A missing limit means "no limit", except `history_days`: its absence means "as `AMBY_SYNC_RETAIN_VERSION_DAYS`" (infinite history would never free the disk). All numbers are integers, bytes without units, **decimal** (1 GB = 1,000,000,000, 20 MB = 20,000,000). The device compares the result of `sealed_body_len(size)`, not the file size, with `max_file_bytes` (П8); the interface shows it in decimal units (as in Finder), so "file up to 20 MB" means what the person sees.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | checked by Opus, 24.10 |
| П51 | **Where it is configured: a single JSON file** `AMBY_SYNC_PLANS_FILE` (by default `<data_dir>/plans.json`; no file — no plans, П52). Shape: `{"default_plan": "standard", "plans": {"standard": {"title": "Стандарт", "max_spaces": 1, "max_total_bytes": 1000000000, "max_file_bytes": 20000000, "history_days": 30, "max_history_bytes": 1000000000}, "plus": {"title": "Плюс", "max_spaces": 10, "max_total_bytes": 10000000000, "max_file_bytes": 200000000, "history_days": 365, "max_history_bytes": 10000000000}, "unlimited": {"title": "Без ограничений"}}}`; the `unlimited` plan (no limits at all) is pinned with the `user plan set` command to the server owner's own account. **Why a file, and not variables or the database.** (a) Variables (П40) carry a nested table badly, and a plan name inside a variable name is confusing. (b) The database stores only the _name_ of the user's plan; the plan definitions in a file can be kept in git, checked by eye, and rolled back by reverting one file. (c) There is no new administrator HTTP interface: the П38 policy is that the administrator works with commands and files. **Strictness.** The file is read at startup; an unknown field, a wrong type, a zero, a repeated name, a `default_plan` without such a plan — **startup refusal** with a clear text (П40): a typo in `max_file_bytes` must not silently remove a limit. A change to the file takes effect on restart (data is safe: Docker `restart`). The command `amby-sync-server plans check [file]` checks the file before the restart. | checked by Opus, 24.10 |
| П52 | **Who has which plan.** `users` gets a new column `plan` (text, may be empty). Empty — "default": the `default_plan` from the file **at that moment** applies (changing `default_plan` changes everyone who is not pinned). Set — pinned by name. If there is no file, there is no `default_plan`: the **virtual plan without limits** applies (all four are absent). **A self-hosted server without a file works exactly as before this application.** A pinned plan that is not in the file — **startup refusal** listing such users (otherwise a typo in the file would silently free or squeeze everyone); It can be cured only by editing the file: `user plan set` **rejects** an unknown plan (code 2), so the only way into such a state is editing the file, and `plans check` before the restart **lists the pinned users whose plan is not in the file being checked**; `user plan set <name> --default` also cures it. A new user (`user add`) gets "default", or `--plan <name>`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                   | checked by Opus, 24.10 |
| П53 | **Administrator commands** (supplement П38; the data is shared with the running server). `user plan set <name> <plan>` — pins; `user plan set <name> --default` — removes the pin; `user plan show <name> [--json]` — the plan, the limits, what is used; `plans list [--json]` — the plans from the file with the number of users on each (and "default"); `plans check [file]` (including the check against pinned users, П52); `space list <name> [--json]` — the user's spaces: `space_id`, creation date, `live_bytes`, `history_bytes` (names stay encrypted); `space remove <name> <space_id> --yes` — **irreversibly** deletes the space with all versions and bodies (without `--yes` it refuses; logged without secrets; in the README: "make a `backup` first"; there is no button in the application until Э5); `user add <name> [--plan <plan>]`; `user list` gets "plan" and "used" columns (in `--json` — new fields, П7). Exit codes: 2 — an unknown plan or a bad name; 1 — no such user and the rest; 0 — success. A plan change is written to the log without secrets (name, previous and new plan). Takes effect immediately: the plan is read from the database on every request.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | checked by Opus, 24.10 |
| П54 | **How the shared settings and the plan relate.** The shared values stay as **server ceilings**: the smaller of the two applies. File: `min(plan, AMBY_SYNC_MAX_BLOB_BYTES)`. Total quota: `min(plan.max_total_bytes, AMBY_SYNC_QUOTA_BYTES_PER_USER)` (empty — the other one). Spaces: the plan only. History: `plan.history_days`, and if it is absent — `AMBY_SYNC_RETAIN_VERSION_DAYS` (П50). So an owner who has not created a file sees the previous behavior; an owner with a file can limit everyone at once with a single number. `/v1/info.limits` stays unchanged (the server ceilings); the **user's** effective limits are in `GET /v1/account` (П61).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | checked by Opus, 24.10 |

### Е.2. What counts toward the quota and how not to get stuck

| No. | Decision                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Note                   |
| --- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------- |
| П55 | **What is counted.** Two numbers per user, across all their spaces together. **"Live bytes" (`live_bytes`)** — the sum of the sizes of **distinct** bodies (`(space, blob_id)` once) referenced by the **latest, non-deleted** version of any entry. **"Pending" (`pending_bytes`)** — bodies referenced by no version: started and completed but not yet part of a commit, by the declared `size` (until cleanup П25). **The quota includes `live_bytes + pending_bytes`.** **Not included:** earlier versions (except when their body is also the body of the latest version — a rename, П30) and deleted entries before cleanup; these bytes are counted separately as `history_bytes` and are only shown. The size is always the encrypted one, as in П42. "Distinct" is counted within one space: an identical `blob_id` in different spaces or for different users is never merged. **Why so.** Deleting or replacing a file in the same commit decreases `live_bytes` — a person at the limit can always free space on their own, without waiting for the 30 days of cleanup.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | checked by Opus, 24.10 |
| П56 | **"Don't get stuck" rules.** (a) **A commit that does not increase `live_bytes` always goes through**, even for a user over the quota (deletion, rename, replacing a file with a smaller one, folders, restoring entries without a new body). (b) **Replacement with credit.** In `POST …/blobs` an optional field `"replaces": hex16` — the `item_id` of the entry whose body is being replaced; the server subtracts the size of its **current** live body from the needed space. So editing a file right at the limit (+4 MB new with the same 4 MB old) does not run into 507. The hint is verified by the server and not trusted: an `item_id` of another user or another space gives no credit (like an unknown entry, with no error); the commit still decides the outcome (c). An old server ignores the field (П7). (c) **The exact check is in the commit.** In the same `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` transaction (П27) the server computes `live_bytes` after applying the batch; if it is greater than `max_total_bytes` **and** greater than `live_bytes` before the batch — `507 quota_exceeded`, the batch is rejected as a whole (as in П27), the state does not change, the uploaded bodies stay pending until cleanup. (d) **The preliminary check in `POST …/blobs`:** a `507` refusal if `live + pending(without this body) − credit + size > max_total_bytes` **and** `size > credit`; an entry smaller than or equal to the one being replaced goes through even over the quota. (e) **`DELETE /v1/spaces/{space_id}/blobs/{blob_id}`** → `204`: deletes the user's **own** body that no version references (including a started one) and frees its bytes immediately; for a body that a version references — `409 blob_exists`; someone else's or nonexistent — `404`. The engine calls it when abandoning an upload (Д75). (f) A repeat of a known body (`200`), parts of a started upload, `complete`, reading — are not checked (as in П42): what has already been accepted is not taken away.                                  | checked by Opus, 24.10 |
| П57 | **Downgrading a plan and reducing limits** (a plan change, an edit of the file, a server ceiling). **Nothing is deleted or compressed.** (a) The quota is already exceeded: growing uploads and commits — `507`; deletion, replacing with a smaller one, rename, reading, downloading from the server — as usual (П56). (b) There are more spaces than allowed: all of them stay fully working, only the creation of a **new** one is blocked (П61); joining an existing one is possible. (c) A file on the server is larger than the new `max_file_bytes`: it stays and can be read; only a **new version** of it of that size is blocked. (d) **History:** the period "earlier versions live `history_days` after replacement" (П35) is now **fixed at the moment of replacement**: `versions` gets a new column `keep_until_ms`, which the server sets when a version is replaced (and at the deletion commit for П36) from the plan **at that moment**. Reducing the plan affects versions replaced **after** the change; those already replaced live out the period promised to them. Cleanup (Б.7) removes by `keep_until_ms`; rows without a value (created before the migration, П58) — by the old rule `superseded_ms + AMBY_SYNC_RETAIN_VERSION_DAYS`. Increasing the plan extends **future** versions; past ones do not come back. **(e) The only exception is the history ceiling `max_history_bytes` (П50).** When the user's `history_bytes` is greater than the ceiling, cleanup removes the **oldest** earlier versions and deleted entries (by replacement or deletion time) ahead of schedule, until it is no greater than the ceiling; **latest versions are never touched**; bodies needed by a latest version stay. This is written into the plan's promise ("history of N days, but no more than M"); without a ceiling there is no exception. The price: reducing the shared `AMBY_SYNC_RETAIN_VERSION_DAYS` no longer frees disk retroactively — this is a decision, not a loss: data leaves no earlier than promised. | checked by Opus, 24.10 |

### Е.3. Database: migration and rollback

| No. | Decision                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Note                   |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------- |
| П58 | **Migration `SCHEMA_V2`** (`MIGRATIONS` gets a second row; `user_version` 1 → 2). Additions only: `ALTER TABLE users ADD COLUMN plan TEXT;` `ALTER TABLE versions ADD COLUMN keep_until_ms INTEGER;` `CREATE INDEX versions_keep ON versions(keep_until_ms) WHERE keep_until_ms IS NOT NULL;`. **Existing data is not rewritten:** for all users `plan` is empty ("default"), for all versions `keep_until_ms` is empty ("by the old rule", П57-г). So a self-hosted server without a file after the update does not differ from the previous one by a single byte of behavior; the quota, however, is counted by the new rule П55 (it only grew in free space). **Before migrating an existing database** (`user_version` ≥ 1 and less than the new one) the server makes a **copy** `VACUUM INTO '<data_dir>/backups/pre-v<N>-<ms>.db'`, verifies it (`quick_check`), and only then runs the migration in one transaction together with `user_version` (as now, `db.rs::migrate`); on any failure the migration is not started, the copy stays. The result is recorded in `meta` (`last_migration`: from which version, to which, when, the copy name), so that a repeated start knows the migration is complete and the person knows where the copy is. **Rollback.** The previous server will refuse to open a version 2 database (`DbError::TooNew`) — this is a protection. Rollback — stop the server, put the copy `backups/pre-v2-*.db` back in place of the database, start the previous image; commits made after the migration are lost (the instructions carry a warning and a "while there is no new data" time frame). Plan assignments are lost on rollback; the previous server does not know them, so everyone is without limits — the safe side. | checked by Opus, 24.10 |

### Е.4. Where it is checked and which responses

The **server** checks; the client checks only in advance, so as not to waste traffic (Д76). The order of checks in `POST …/blobs`: token → shape of fields → size (`413`) → "the body already exists" (`200`/`409`, П21) → quota (`507`).

| No. | Decision                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            | Note                   |
| --- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------- |
| П59 | **Three cases — three different codes**, the client tells them apart only by `code`: **"file too large" — `413 blob_too_large`** (the existing code), now `details: {"size": N, "max_bytes": N, "source": "plan" \| "server"}`; **"quota full" — `507 quota_exceeded`** (existing), `details: {"used_bytes": N, "limit_bytes": N, "needed_bytes": N}` (`used` = `live + pending`, `needed` — how much is missing, taking the credit into account); **"enough spaces" — `403 space_limit`** (new), `details: {"max_spaces": N, "spaces": N}`. The messages (`message`) are for people, the client does not parse them (П4); the texts of the codes are in the application's `sync.errors.*`.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                         | checked by Opus, 24.10 |
| П60 | **Check points.** (1) `POST /v1/spaces` (П20): if the user already has `max_spaces` spaces — `403 space_limit`, nothing is created; a repeat of an already created `space_id` — as before `409 space_exists` (checked earlier than the limit). (2) `POST …/blobs` (П21): `size` > the effective `max_file_bytes` — `413 blob_too_large`; then the quota — `507` (П56-г). (3) `POST …/commit` (П26): growth of `live_bytes` beyond the quota — `507` (П56-в); an entry version whose body is larger than the effective `max_file_bytes` (the entry references an already uploaded large body after a plan downgrade) — `413 blob_too_large`, the batch is rejected as a whole. Not checked: `GET` of everything, parts and `complete` (П56-е), `GET /v1/spaces` and entering an existing space. All checks are in the same transaction as the write (П27); the race of two devices is decided by the order of transactions, and no check is made "by eye" before it. | checked by Opus, 24.10 |

### Е.5. Account endpoint

| No. | Decision                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | Note                   |
| --- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------- |
| П61 | **`GET /v1/account`** (a token is required) → `200`: `{"plan": {"name": "standard" \| null, "title": "Стандарт" \| null, "pinned": bool}, "limits": {"max_spaces": N \| null, "max_total_bytes": N \| null, "max_file_bytes": N \| null, "history_days": N}, "usage": {"live_bytes": N, "pending_bytes": N, "history_bytes": N, "spaces": N}, "spaces": [{"space_id", "name_enc", "key_epoch", "wrapped_vk", "seq", "created_ms", "live_bytes": N, "history_bytes": N}]}`. `limits` are **effective** (П54: the smaller of the plan and the server ceiling); `null` — no limit; `history_days` is always a number. Without a plans file `plan.name` equals `null`, `limits` — only the server ceilings. The `spaces` rows are the same as in `GET /v1/spaces` (П20), plus sizes; names stay encrypted, the application decrypts them. `history_bytes` — earlier versions and deleted entries before cleanup, **counted separately and not included in the quota** (П55). A server that does not have this path (older than the application) answers `404 not_found` — the client then behaves as before and does not show the limits (Р13). The number is computed by a query over the indexes `versions_feed` and `versions_blob`; counter caches are introduced only if a measurement shows the query is slow (open question 7). | checked by Opus, 24.10 |
| П62 | **Server version.** `GET /v1/info` (П9) returns in `server_version` the version of the `sync/server` crate (`CARGO_PKG_VERSION`); the crate is bumped together with this work (for example, to `0.2.0`), `protocol` stays `{min: 1, max: 1}`. Currently `0.0.0` arrives.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | checked by Opus, 24.10 |

### Е.6. Engine (Д decisions)

`Remote` gets `account()` (П61) and an optional credit in `blob_start(blob, size, replaces: Option<&ItemId>)`, and `blob_delete(blob)` (П56-д). State on the device **is not added**: all decisions are recomputed on every pass from fresh data (like the "not synced" list), so "retry when the limits have changed" requires no special mechanism.

| No. | Decision                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                  | Note                   |
| --- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ---------------------- |
| Д73 | **The pass takes the limits at step 2** (after `info`, Д10): `account()`; from it — `PassLimits {max_file_bytes (effective), free_bytes = max_total − live − pending, quota_full}`. If it could not be obtained (`404` — an old server, the network) — only `info.max_blob_bytes` applies and no preliminary quota checks are made; a network error does not stop the pass (the server's checks will still fire). The engine **does not store** the limits between passes.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                | checked by Opus, 24.10 |
| Д74 | **A file that is too large — only here.** A new or modified file of one's own with `sealed_body_len(size)` > `PassLimits.max_file_bytes` is not sent and **not deleted**; it stays on disk as is, lands in `blocked` (Д12) and in "not synced" with the reason `TooLarge { max_bytes, source }` (field `source`: `plan`/`server`; the existing `TooLarge` is extended, the order of reasons: `ExcludedPath` → `TooBigForMemory` (Д71) → `TooLarge`). Its previous version on the server **is not changed and not deleted**, copies on other devices stay. From then on it is an ordinary locally modified file: if someone else's edit of this entry arrives from the server, the rules of the Р7 table apply (a conflict, both versions kept). If the limits grew or the file was made smaller, the next pass will send it by itself. **A plan downgrade does not turn already synced files into deleted ones:** a file on the server that is larger than the new limit but has not changed locally is not touched at all.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                               | checked by Opus, 24.10 |
| Д75 | **Quota full — sending stops, receiving goes on.** Before sending a file the pass computes the space needed: `body_size − credit`, where the credit is the size of the entry's current body per the feed (`blob_size`), if the entry exists. Within a pass, everything the pass has already sent is subtracted from `free_bytes` (otherwise every file would pass the preliminary check and the server would reject the later ones). If it does not fit in the remainder of `free_bytes` — the file is not sent (reason `QuotaFull`, stays on disk, in `blocked`); otherwise it is sent with `replaces` (П56-б). If the server nevertheless answered `507 quota_exceeded` (a race, another device) — the same result for this file, **the pass is not aborted**: the other, smaller files are tried; the request for the same file is not repeated in this pass. Commits that do not grow in body (deletions, renames, folders, Д24) go as always. All incoming changes (Д17) are applied as always. An upload that was started but abandoned (the file changed or was deleted, `RemoveUpload`) is freed by a `blob_delete` call **where possible**: an error in the call is not a pass error (cleanup П25 will remove it after 24 hours anyway). Space on the server was freed (the person deleted files, the administrator raised the plan) — the next pass, with a fresh `account()`, will send what was waiting. What has been sent is never deleted. | checked by Opus, 24.10 |
| Д76 | **The client checks in advance, the server finally.** The preliminary checks of Д74 and Д75 exist only so as not to push megabytes for nothing; they can be wrong in both directions (the `account()` data went stale during the pass). If they err toward sending — the server will refuse (`413`/`507`), and the result is the same as that of a preliminary refusal; if they err toward refusing — the file waits one pass. The rule: **any refusal by a limit, preliminary or from the server, gives the same thing — the file stays on disk, is listed with a reason, and is tried again on the next pass.** The codes `413 blob_too_large` and `507 quota_exceeded` from `blob_start` and from `commit` (П60) are no longer unknown errors (`Rejected`): they turn into the reasons `TooLarge` and `QuotaFull`. A `commit` rejected as a whole by `507` (П56-в) does not lose entries: the bodies are already on the server, the rows stay in the send queue, and a new attempt goes with the next pass; the record of what is being sent (С2, Д7) is kept until success.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                           | checked by Opus, 24.10 |
| Д77 | **The space limit concerns creation, not joining.** `POST /v1/spaces` answers `403 space_limit`; the engine does not take part in it (creating a space is in the application, `space.rs`). A device joining an **existing** space (`open_space`) is not affected by the limit. The application, on receiving `space_limit`, creates nothing and offers to connect an existing space (У3).                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 | checked by Opus, 24.10 |
| Д78 | **Interaction with protections and conflicts.** (a) **File size (Д71):** stays, the first of the reasons. (b) **Conflicts (Г):** a conflict copy is an ordinary new file; if it does not fit, it stays on disk and in the conflict set (Г19–Г25), the conflict record is not lost; a merge result that does not fit stays a local file, and the server keeps the previous one. (c) **Mass changes (Д54):** files not sent because of a limit are **not deleted locally** and are not part of "outgoing"; a plan downgrade after which many files at once became "too large" is not considered a mass change. Deleting a file that remained only here sends nothing to the server while the entry has no version on the server. (d) **Stops (Д60):** a limit is not a stop; `Stopped` is not used.
